> **Open work lives in the OPEN WORK section at the end of this file** (re-consolidated 2026-10-11 evening). Items marked [→] elsewhere were folded into it.

[x] - empty land should have more nature, rocks and sand for deseart landscape and trees/grass for those areas. high-value areas can look like parklets clean cut grass, city land taken care of. but empty lots in bad value areas can lay dorment, overgrown and trashy.   → DONE: vacant lots dress by land value (trashy / wild / parklet), rocks and scrub scattered on open ground (nd_procgen::vacant, terrain::scatter_nature).
[x] - we need other special city things like police stations, fire stations, hospitals, schools ect. and buildings within the three tiers for all these, is this possible? is our building generator robust enough for these, if not we need to work on that tool a bit more to handle new buildings we will add in the future. we'll also need veichels and emergencies that they travel to. people can get sick, buildings can get on fire or crimes can happen, someone would need to call 911 for a person working at dispatch to send out a team so all that system should be in play. special buildings are user-placed. also need some special attractions like minuments and stadiums. make a "buildings" tool that we can add these buildings have sub-catagories for different things put "power" as a sub category.   → DONE: police, fire, health, school, 911 dispatch, jail, monuments and a stadium in three tiers; fires, crime and sickness answered by vehicles with sirens; out-of-town units when uncovered; a Buildings tool with Power, Services, Transit, Rail, Water and Attractions categories.
[x] - add a "zones" toggle to show/hide the set zoning on the map  → DONE: Zoning outlines checkbox and the O key.
[x] - when you "inspect" somthing it should be highlighted to indicate what is selected.  → DONE: selected building lot outlined, ring under the selected person or car.
[x] - clicking on a car or person should show their path indicating their destination. and their route so far. if i click a person I should see a person-pane draggable popup with their info, history, home, work and desitnation and other info about them. we shoud also be able to "favorite" that person for a run to jump back to them.    → DONE: remaining route, trail so far, destination lot; draggable person pane with history, home, work, destination; favourites list with jump-to.
[x] - if I resize a road, it might change a parcel size, that can't just destroy the sidewalk the building has got to go, city emminent domain to change a road. currently the buidings just stay and the sidewalk goes under the building when I expanded a road thats not legal and the buidling gotta change. even if I build a path where a building previously stood that building should go and the parcel should adjust for the new path. this should happen any time a parcel size/shape changes underneath a building that building ususally has some new issue and doesn't fit well in the new spot so its gotta go.   → DONE: eminent domain: a building whose parcel changes shape (area ±12% or outline moved >3 m) is pulled down on implement; residents remember it.
[x] - construction/destruction of buildings should take time and visually be displayed on the building tiles. we should never have buildings popping up out of no-where. vusally show the construction process with truss and building stuff and trucks deliverying supplies and construction workers coming into town or who live in town working on it.   → DONE: buildings go up over 1-12 days as visible sites (hoarding, raft, shell, scaffold, crane, cabin, mixer, workers), four material deliveries by truck, crew jobs; homes and jobs open on completion. Demolition is still instant.
[x] - let's rework the UI think about how other city builders do it, buttons for main tools and sub panes for tool parameters. new pop-up windows for info panes, market pane, city info pane, population info panes ect. add a "income inequality" graph displaying population over income   → DONE: top bar (clock, speed, treasury, population, window toggles, camera), bottom toolbar with one button per tool and a sub-pane for the active tool's settings, draggable City/Economy/Market/People/Power/Transit/Land/Saves/Help windows; People has an income histogram by class with a Lorenz curve and Gini.
[x] - im seeing some low-teir stacks that were built on day one sitting in a new high-value area next to other tall buildings of high-level. this stack should have raised prices since the land value is now 1.0 and pushed out all the poorer residents long ago. also we should have different sizes of building based on that density of that area at the time of the build so high-rises can only be built if its aready a highly trafficed and high-density area, otherwise the homes/buildings are smaller and single story. this allows for buildings of different heights to form dynamically based on the popularion and need.   → DONE: low buildings on high-value land are redeveloped at the land's tier after 3 days; heights capped by local density; households now choose homes they can afford and move somewhere cheaper when rent climbs.
[x] - can we have a zone paint brush. when you brush it creates a parcel under the mouse with that zone. parcels can only be valid based on our ruleset. i think easiest implimentation is to always have parcels cut even before zoning is done since its deterministic based on the road, then user can define zone on each parcel with a brush or the existing range tool or like a markquee tool could be cool. then the parcels can color to their selected zone.    → DONE: parcels are cut along every road before zoning; the Zone tool has Brush (right-drag erases), Marquee and Polygon modes, parcels refuse uses the ruleset forbids and colour by their (planned) zone; several named projects with their own undo, previewed dim (Projects dropdown).
[x] - this city needs more graffiti im not seeing enough and that adds a lot of grid to it. even LA has a tower with graff all over it now in 2026! did you know that? lower value places gotta have more, stacks are prime targets for street-artists and they wont get cleaned by the city. Overall textures should be a bit more gritty, outdoor stairs are rusted, roads can be run-down in bad areas, cracked sidewalks and details at closer range.   → DONE: graffiti, rusted stairs, potholes and cracked slabs, plus seven worn texture variants (stains, drips, cracks, rust bleed, crazed paving) used on low-tier and worn buildings and run-down streets.
[x] - add more building styles types and variations for all types of structures. the height of the building can also change it's formfactor. I see a number of structures that just look too similar, we need to add more variation to our generative tool and add more building options to the field. look at hong-kong and akira and ghost in the shell for example buildings. these "castle" buildings are weird and should be more glass-castles and less phallic. maybe every building style can have adjacency scores, so some buildings styles go well close together (think tall residential in shanghai) vs spires in downtown districts might want to look more distinct to next-door buildings. stacks can always be close together so that can also pressure the city to build more interesting structures.    → DONE: 12 new styles (Kowloon walled block, HK pencil tower, Shanghai twin slabs, Akira megastructure, GitS glass terraces, capsule hotel, hillside steps, night market, neon tower, workshops, flatted factory, fab plant), forms that change with floors, glass castles instead of spires, adjacency families so stacks cluster and spires stay distinct.
[x] - hover info: when hovering over a parcel show stats, its zone, what building is there if any, ect.   → DONE: parcel hover card with land use, zone, area, land value, building tier/floors/homes/jobs/business.
[x] - if a building is placed whatever previous buildings that were on affected parcels need to go. if a spcial builing clips a parcel it needs to re-size. special buildings of any type need to prevent other properties from using that parcel. it overides the zoning say if a power plant is put down our sim shoudn't overlapp it with a warehouse obviously but i did see one instance of a shop built underneath a trainstation so thats one instance we should make sure never happens.   → DONE: specials own their ground: lots under a facility footprint are removed, the city buys out and pulls down any grown building (homes, shops, sites) on the ground it places a plant, depot, station or yard on; another civic structure must be bulldozed first; development never builds under a footprint.
[x] - review the walking mode and make sure things look ok. if we need to add more street level detail (even if it only renders in this view) that is ok to do. pov view should look cool enough to screenshot in certain places.    → DONE: walk mode reviewed by screenshot and fixed; street-level detail spawns only near the walker (signage, vending machines, food carts, steam, cables and lanterns, neon puddles, scooters, clutter).
[x] - did I see helepads? we should definitely have drone taxis for the rich thats definitely in our distopian future.    → DONE: vertiports (pad, vertiport, skyport) under Transit; rooftop helidecks on the richest towers by demand; drone taxis for the rich when pads serve both ends; air ambulances; a police helicopter with a searchlight; drone noise lowers land value.
[x] -topology and water. we should have a river nearby the city then some variation in landscape, we can choose a landscape at the "new game..." page that displays optional landscapes with levers to adjust values like river or islands or how much water or height variation. we'll also need shipping ports and ferry ports where automatically routes can be connected to "elsewhere" on the map as well as any other docks we build. must be built near the water and builds out a pier for boats to connect to. there should always be one major highway at the start of the game, traffic always flows through that and the city can build on/off ramps for it. rivers can sometimes branch into smaller ones or streams.  streams and rivers have different currents and can be used for hydro power or other boat related things. (shipping/ferries)   → DONE: landscape generator (river valley, islands, coast, delta, plains, flat) with rivers, streams, lakes, sea and hot spots; New Game window with presets, sliders and a mini-map; roads follow the ground and bridge water; a highway edge to edge with through traffic, joined by ramps; shipping ports and ferry terminals with piers, freighters and ferries to elsewhere. Embankment/underpass polish in progress.
[x] - power plants, we can add some more unique ones like geo-thermal (if ground allows), tidal waves, hydro-power (might need some mechanics to bridge over rivers or streams).   → DONE: hydro (needs river current), tidal (coast, two tides a day) and geothermal (hot spots), placed with site checks.

[x] - comprehensive code review of the whole project   → DONE: docs/REVIEW.md consolidates four parallel reviews (sim core, networks/geometry, procgen, app); 19 verified defects fixed in the same commit (long-frontage parcelling, stale rail segment panic, roads through junctions, lost truck cargo, NaN vertices, egui focus re-click, right-drag committing gestures, ...), the rest ranked with a suggested order of work.

[x] - cars freezing for hours just after turning onto a roundabout   → DONE (root cause): rings are made of ~9 m pieces; a car moving into the next piece could land on the tail of the car just ahead, the overlap fix shoved the car behind back to the start of its piece, and cars piled up there (ring 2 of the player's city held 107 cars on 490 m, 35 overlapping). Cars now wait at the end of their piece until there is room; a car squeezed back past the start goes back onto the lane behind, and one standing right on another pulls aside and rejoins when there is room. On the player's snapshot the piles clear at once and the selected car drives off.

- [x] Stray see-through coloured blocks with every layer off (Harvey 2026-10-27, screenshot of the day-2 city around the cloverleaf interchange): these are the plan hologram's raised zone fills (`raised_zone` in nd_app render/preview.rs), drawn for every parcel whose planned zone differs from its built zone, with or without a project or layer. In his city 23 parcels are zoned in the master plan but never got a lot (15 residential, 4 civic, 3 commercial, 1 entertainment, around x -150..50, y 110..330; all on a street, not rough, the use accepted; no projects exist), so their hologram never goes away. Find why implement made no lot for them (small slivers between ramps? 107–793 m²) and either make the lots or drop them from the plan with a refusal; and only draw the hologram for an active or parked project, or with the Zone tool or zoning layer. → DONE [p1-sim-leftovers]: cause: all 23 lie under the cloverleaf's ramps where a deck passes too low for a storey (or a column stands): implement made their lots, then the rebuild dropped every lot under a low deck or on a column (`rebuild_from_master_with`, the [tools] pillar/deck pass) and left the parcels zoned in the plan (and naming the lost lot), so the plan never matched what was built. Now a parcel under a deck too low for a storey or with a column on it is marked (`Parcel::under_deck`, `World::mark_under_decks` whenever parcels are cut or loaded) and refuses every use but a park ("a raised road runs too low over this ground (or its columns stand here): nothing can be built under it", shown by the Zone tool like any refusal), so painting or an area zone leaves it out of the plan and old plans that zoned it drop it on load; a joined lot dropped for a column frees its parcels. The raised fills now show only while a project is open or parked, or with the Zone tool or the zoning layer (`sync_preview`). Checked with `examples/stray_zone_probe` on a copy of his day-2 save: 42 planned-but-unbuilt parcels → 17 (the 23 under the ramps and 2 backland under them gone; the 17 left are backland his older exe zoned without lots, which the current code lots on the next implement: after a rebuild 0); screenshots O:/nd_targets/p1-sim-leftovers/run1/screenshots/stray_before.png → stray_after.png (layers off; stray_after_zoning.png with the zoning layer). Test `tools_tests::ground_under_a_low_deck_is_never_left_zoned_without_a_lot`. Left: no lot under the ramps (a car park or yard under an overpass would be the real-life use).

- [x] Building detail from Harvey's inspiration pictures (Harvey 2026-10-27: "I would love to get this level of detail in our buildings but also procedural"). 38 reference renders in `Inspirations/` (Hong_Kong/ 14, cyberpunk/ 24; jpg and webp, both readable). Style and detail reference only: never trace or copy them, everything stays procedural (no hand-made assets), stylised and gritty, details permanent and the same from every camera. First step: look at every picture and write `docs/STYLE_REFERENCE.md` listing the recurring details per family with which of our styles each feeds and how to build it procedurally. Seen so far:
  - **Hong Kong tenements** (feeds Kowloon stacks / The Walled Quarter, Lantern Alley): tall slab towers on a 1–2 storey podium; a deep ledge over the shops; a dense window grid with a different frame per flat; AC units hung under most windows, laundry poles and caged balconies; side wings with a vertical stair or lift core, often with drainpipes and a ladder-like run of pipes; mixed facades on one building (stained pink tile next to bare concrete); damp and rust streaks; rooftop clutter (antennas, water tanks, satellite dishes, rooftop shacks, railings); shopfronts with roller shutters, coloured awnings, big billboards on the podium and vertical neon signs on the corners.
  - **Cyberpunk blocks** (feeds Neon strip, Capsule, Skyline Luxe, Glass Campus, offices and industry): chamfered concrete with panel seams; cantilevered glass-box floors stacked out of the frame; tall louvred vent panels and big fans; exposed stair cores with diagonal bracing; open steel frames with holographic text signs running round them; lit glass storeys with screens inside; railings round every roof and terrace; antennas, dishes and rooftop plant; a big lit brand sign on the facade; street-level detail around the plot (bollards, barriers, traffic lights, parking pylon, bins).
  - Needs the L1 graphics foundation for the far-off cost (detail fades with distance; instancing for the AC units and the rest) and feeds the L2 looks.
  → DONE (style guide + Hong Kong pass) [r4-style]: `docs/STYLE_REFERENCE.md` (all 38 pictures by file name: details per family, which looks they feed, how to build each, cost per building, the LOD rules). Walled blocks and pencil towers (buildings/hk.rs) get a deep ledge over the shops, roller shutters with drums, striped awnings, fascia signs, a frame per flat (mullion/transom/sill varying by a hash of seed, face, floor and flat), many more AC units (window and split) with damp streaks, steel cages, tile bands (mixed facades), a stair/lift core up a side of the pencil tower with pipe ladders and a motor room, pipe runs on walled-block sides, corner neon, a podium billboard, roof railings and TV aerials; all from a separate stream so the buildings' mass, windows and signs stand where they did. Level of detail: `Geometry::detail` (Lod::Mid to 700 m, Lod::Near to 320 m) spawned per 64 m cell with Bevy `VisibilityRange` (shadows too); glow always stays in the base mesh. `status.render` gains `detail_triangles`, `detail_in_range`, `drawn_triangles`. Left: the cyberpunk family (section 2 of the guide) for Neon strip, offices and industry; street props round the plot; Lantern Alley, Skyline Luxe and Glass Campus looks; the other HK-ish styles (twin slabs, Stacks, capsule hotel) are not dressed yet.
- [x] Building viewer (Harvey 2026-10-27: "look at all the buildings in the city in a sort of 3D viewer, flip through and look at them and then I can leave notes on them"). A window (or full-screen mode) that shows one building at a time on a turntable against a plain backdrop, as the inspiration renders do: orbit, zoom, day/night toggle, lights on/off. Flip through with arrows or the keyboard; filter by style/form, land use, district/story, business kind and tier; two lists: the buildings standing in the city, and the catalogue of every style the generator can make (seeded variants). Shows the name, style, form, floors, tenants and owner. A note box per building or per style: notes are saved (in ND_HOME, not the save) and can be read and answered through the remote API (like the F8 flags), so Claude and agents can pick them up as work items. Optional: a side-by-side with an inspiration picture. → DONE [r4-viewer]: top bar "Viewer" (or X) opens a window with the building on a turntable (its own stage, lights, fog and glow material: drag to turn, wheel to zoom, Turn, Day/Night, Lights, Save picture); tabs City buildings / Catalogue (all 151 styles, 3 seeds x tiers x heights) / Notes; filters by search, style group, form, land use, district, story, business, tier, open notes; name, style, form, floors, footprint, district, owner, tenants (city) or key, section, variant, seed (catalogue); "Go to it in the city"; notes per building and per style in ND_HOME/building_notes.ron with replies and done/reopen; an Inspirations picture beside it; remote `viewer` and `building_notes` (docs/REMOTE.md). - [ ] Islands reached only by ferry (Harvey 2026-10-27: "I want some people to be ok living on an island or commuting there ... some islands may just have a big ferry for the only access"). Today (checked in code): ferries carry only visitors, day-trippers and commuters from towns off the map (world/ferry.rs); residents' everyday trips (`travel_with`, world/trips/travel.rs) never board a ferry between two terminals in town, and world/connectivity.rs marks any walk/drive component that is not the main one as a stranded area, ferry or not. So islanders complain about the pedestrian network and cannot commute. Plan:
  - **Ferries are a trip mode:** walk (or bus) to the terminal, wait on the pier for the next boat, ride, walk on, as bus and metro legs do; fare paid through the ledger; the planner weighs the wait and sailing time; last boat home matters (night shifts need a late boat).
  - **Connectivity counts ferries (and air taxis):** a component whose terminal has a route to the main network is "connected by ferry" (an Island, shown on the Walk networks layer in its own colour), not stranded; the complaint becomes ferry-specific (long waits, full boats, no late boat) instead of "no pedestrian network".
  - **Car ferry / ro-ro:** a big ferry that carries cars and lorries, so deliveries, construction materials, tow and trash trucks and residents' cars reach islands with no bridge; slow and limited (a queue at the ramp).
  - **Island life as a choice:** households weigh commute time in choosing a home, so some accept the ferry (retirees, people who work on the island, those who like quiet; island homes cheaper, or dearer for a nice island). Island shops and jobs serve islanders; growth on an island is slower while access is poor.
  - **Services:** emergency cover on an island needs a station there, a fireboat or the air ambulance; otherwise a coverage warning, not a network complaint.

- [x] Buildings grow under raised paths and roads built before the zoning (Harvey 2026-10-28, Shanghai-style raised walkways over roundabouts, zoned underneath afterwards; on his day-9 save two older raised paths at about (-52,193)–(-97,187) now have buildings under spans longer than the 42 m a span may be, shown red). Hotfix on main 5e9323e: such old gaps no longer refuse every project (only gaps on or under the project's own roads do). Still to do: growth and placement must keep an existing deck's pillar spots and clearance free: build around the columns, and under the deck only where the building fits below it (low shops, car parks, markets under a walkway, like Shanghai or Hong Kong); a building taller than the clearance must not stand under the deck. Decide what happens to the buildings already under his decks (pull down only those that block a pillar or reach the deck, or add the missing columns where there is room), and stop the red gap markers showing for old roads that stand anyway. → DONE [r4-decks]: the "buildings" under his two walkways were parks (Saffron Plaza, Aurora Memorial, Lotus Memorial, Onyx Greenway...) zoned on days 7.7-8.1 under the 6 m decks, the only use ground under a deck that low takes, on the ground of the columns; a column treated a park as something the city keeps, so every rebuild found no room for those columns (gaps (-52,193)-(-97,187) and (-60,239)-(-145,238)). Now a column stands in a park like on open ground (`pillar_protected`), and an old raised road keeps the columns it stood on in any stretch that has no room for a new one (unless a road now runs there: `place_pillars`, `pillars::on_stretch`/`stretch_held`), so no red span is left on a road that stands anyway. His save needs no pull-downs: no grown building stands under a deck or on a column; its 42 columns stay where they are and a project anywhere gets no gap. Under a deck what goes up fits below it: new buildings and malls, redevelopments and rebuilds (over all merged plots), estate houses, car parks and campus annexes are held to the clearance (`deck_fit`, `deck_cap_plots`); no tower, HQ or merged plot takes a lot under a deck (`under_a_deck`); the Buildings tool refuses a column's ground and anything taller than the clearance (`deck_problem`; a surface car park fits under any deck, a one-storey post under a 12 m one). Low uses first: homes grow there at half the chance, an office opens as a shop or market stall when one is viable, a car park counts a lot under a deck 80 m nearer. Tests `world/deck_ground_tests.rs`; probe `examples/deck_probe.rs`. Left: the rest of "Layered city" (kiosks/market props under decks, people sheltering there, many levels)

- [ ] Layered city: life under and between raised roads (Harvey 2026-10-28: "in our future city I might want to layer more and more paths vertically ... Life can exist on a lower level of traffic underneath the infrastructure"). References he gave: Hong Kong's Mid-Levels hillside escalators and elevated walkways; LA's giant highway overpasses and stacked interchanges; New York's big bridges with parks, cafés and walks beneath them (e.g. the parks under the Brooklyn and Manhattan Bridges); in LA, homeless people shelter and build camps under bridges and raised ways. Today a raised road only plans pillars and refuses ground it is too low over (P1 leftovers: `Parcel::under_deck` takes parks only). Plan:
  - **Ground under a deck is a place, not a void:** the clearance under each deck is known per spot; ground there can hold what fits: parks and plazas, cafés and kiosks, markets and food stalls, car parks, bike parks, depots and yards, skate parks and courts, low shops under a high viaduct. Growth builds around the columns and only as tall as the clearance; nothing taller than the deck stands under it (pairs with the "buildings grow under raised paths" bug).
  - **Many levels:** walkways, streets and rail can stack several levels deep (ground, +1 walkway, +2 street, highway above); stairs, escalators (Hong Kong style, one-way by time of day) and lifts join the levels; buildings get doors and shopfronts on the level a walkway meets them (a sky-bridge entrance on floor 3), so a raised walkway can be a high street of its own.
  - **People use it:** walkers pick levels by route; shade and shelter under decks (rain, heat) draw people to sit, eat and meet there; the space under a big bridge or overpass gets its own mood (dark, loud, sheltered) that a park or lights can lift.
  - **The homeless shelter there:** with nowhere to live, people make camps under bridges and raised ways (tents, tarps, shopping carts, fires in barrels at night); camps grow and shrink with homelessness, can be cleared (by policy, with a mood and crime cost) or helped (shelters, outreach); ties to the Riverbank Shantytown story and the Patchwork look.
  - **Look:** the underside reads as its own world: deck soffits with lights, columns with posters and graffiti, pigeons, drips and stains, lit stalls; the same from every camera.
  - Needs the parcel/plan rules for 3D ground (clearance per parcel), the walk graph across levels, procgen for under-deck uses and multi-level entrances, and the L4 Underground work for the levels below ground.

- [x] Pressure "held: no vacant lot that suits it" should say what lot suits it (Harvey 2026-10-28 asked how to satisfy "Fish gutting shed 0 in town, 8.0 wanted"): name the land use, minimum area, street need and the anchor and reach from its `Placement` (world/pressure.rs), e.g. "needs a vacant industrial lot of 500 m² or more on a street within 1.4 km of a fishing port", and offer a "show where" that highlights the qualifying area on the map. → DONE [r4-hunger]: `World::lot_need(kind)` (`LotNeed`: land uses, min area, street, waterfront, anchors, reach, what the anchors are) and `lot_need_lots(kind)` (the vacant lots of its land uses near the reach, each `LotFit`: suits / taken / not on the water / no jetty room / too small / on a footpath / too far; `pressure_lot` now asks the same `lot_fit`, same order, same pick). Held reads e.g. "no vacant lot that suits it: it needs a vacant industrial lot of 800 m² or more on a street within 1.5 km of a livestock farm (no vacant industrial lot nearby)" or "(vacant commercial or entertainment lots: 17 not on the water, 2 too far)". City Data, Economy, Overview: a "show where" / "hide" button under a pressure held for want of a lot (flies the camera to its first anchor); the map (`nd_app/src/pressure_where.rs`, gizmos) puts a post and the reach ring at each anchor (a soft 300 m ring when it has no reach), outlines each vacant lot of its land uses by fit (green suits, amber too small, orange footpath, blue not on the water, grey too far) with a post, and a banner says it in words. API: every pressure reading has `needs` {text, uses, min_area_m2, street, waterfront, near, anchors, reach_m}; `pressure_where {kind}` / `{off: true}` (alias `show_where`) shows it and returns `lots_by_fit` and `lots`. Checked on a copy of Harvey's day-15 save (port 4932): beach bar, beach resort and slaughterhouse held lines, screenshots O:/nd_targets/r4-hunger/run3/screenshots (beach_bar_where.png, econ_pane.png). Tests `a_lot_need_reads_as_what_to_zone`, `a_pressure_held_for_want_of_a_lot_says_what_lot`

- [x] Paths joined to a street chop it into tiny junctions; crossings pile up; riders fly (Harvey 2026-10-28, flag 1 in target/release/flags/claude_flags.jsonl, day 16 02:25 at (-60,300)). Car 208:189 (Ilya Fontaine) on the road 654 min against 201: its route crosses junction nodes 50–56 about every 10 m (road pieces 3.4–8.8 m), each footbridge stair foot or path end joined to the street adding a junction and a crossing; a sedan floats over the verge and bikes "fly up in the air instead of using the paths". Harvey: "I was adding many pathways around, connecting sidewalks with streets in random places. Not all of those ending spots was supposed to generate a crosswalk ... when adding the second path connected to the road, it should see a nearby crosswalk or footbridge on that same road and not add an additional crosswalk a few feet away"; and "walkers should prioritize an overpass nearby instead of a crosswalk if they can". Fix: a path meeting a street joins the pavement, not the carriageway (no new road junction, no new crossing) when a crossing or footbridge on that street is near (merge into it); never split a street into pieces shorter than a minimum; walkers prefer a footbridge or underpass within a short detour over an at-grade crossing; riders and cars stay on their own surfaces (no climbing footbridge stairs or floating); clean up his save's extra junctions and crossings on load. → DONE [r4-crossings]: the spot is the outer ring (r 120 m) of his triple roundabout; nodes 51–54 on the car's route are the ring's own 10 m curve vertices (plain bends, no junction behaviour), the real junctions are 46/48/50/55. Causes: (1) his underpass path (at −10 m) passes under the ring 1.9 m from junction 48, and the way above got a vertex there, cutting a 1.9 m stub off the ring and a 1.4 m one off the spoke (shorter than the junction, so the lanes there had no length: a car on the 1.4 m stub was drawn 2.2 m off the road, and the moves round that corner were erratic); now no vertex closer than `roads::MIN_PIECE` (6 m) to a node; (2) a path end dropped on a street cut it wherever it landed, so two paths to either pavement made two cuts and two crossings; now a new path end joins the street's node when it lands within 6 m of one or within `PATH_MEETS` (8 m) of another path's meeting point (`RoadNetwork::path_end_join`, applied when the path is drawn, so paths already in a city lay as before and no building moves); (3) crossings piled up: a path's crossing over a ring was never closed by K15 (only zebras were) and two junctions 20 m apart each set a ring crossing back towards the other (three within 3.6 m at junctions 48–50); now ring crossings count in K15 and the second of two crosswalks of neighbouring junctions within `CROSSING_MIN_GAP` 8 m over the same street is closed; (4) walkers: a crosswalk within `BRIDGE_DRAW` 50 m of a footbridge or of a path passing under/over the same street costs `BRIDGED_GRADE_PENALTY` (`Crossing::near_bridge`), so they go a short way round to it; (5) riders: cyclists already wheel bikes over footbridges (Harvey's earlier ask) but were drawn riding up the stairs; on a raised walkway leg they are now drawn on foot (`World::wheeling_bike`). Old saves: roads are laid again from the plan on load when the plan lays them differently (`relay_roads_after_load`, traffic carried), and crossing upgrades whose crosswalk moved go to the one that took its place within 12 m (`reanchor_crossing_rules`): his save loses the two stubs (and one more at (397,205)), keeps all 4 footbridges, 548 buildings and 675 lots. His day-16 save, 24 h from 02:25, within 60 m of (−60,305), main → branch: street pieces under 5 m 2 → 0; cars standing >60 s mean 2.6 → 0.0 (max 23 → 3; queued car-ticks 2,182 → 35, held by jaywalkers 241 → 3, at kerb 793 → 0); cars through 1,411 → 1,512, mean time in the spot 10.6 → 4.2 min; walkers crossing at grade 1,561 → 500, up on a footbridge 122 → 313. Ilya's 654 min was spent before the save (she was 10 min from the shop; arrives at 21755 both ways). Shots O:/nd_targets/r4-crossings/run1/screenshots/before_*.png, after_*.png. Left: riders still ride up and down the steep ramps Harvey drew (the raised path's 6 m in 35 m, the underpass's 10 m in 12 m) as drawn; a button rule on a crosswalk the redundancy rule closes is not reopened. Tests `world/r4_crossings_tests.rs` (8)

- [ ] A big world: seamless edges, an expandable map, a planet (Harvey 2026-10-28; discussed, plan agreed in outline, details to settle as each step starts). Harvey: "The extremities of the playable map should extend seamlessly outward. Ideally the map could extend indefinitely. I could make sister cities with an entire city being one district ... build an entire planet ... presets map types are not adjustable with a seed but instead specific spots on the globe! then we can choose a starting point but eventually take over the planet". Decisions: the planet has other towns already (simulated as numbers: population, what they make and need; real places with names and lights that trade with us); it should feel big and expansive; the colony / empty-planet hard mode and the globe/space camera come later.
  1. [x] **Seamless far landscape** (do first, about a day): the same generator makes terrain, coast and sea out to the horizon at low detail in fog, so a Coast map has no sharp water edge; highways, rail and shipping lanes run visibly off into it; neighbouring towns show as distant skylines that light up at night. Nothing out there is simulated. → DONE [r4-horizon]: `nd_procgen::horizon`. `FarLand` is a pure function of the saved map (seed, params, its rivers, coast and islands) and world coordinates: the coast runs on east and west past where the map's sea polygon stopped (bays and headlands from the seed, never north of the coast's northernmost point, so never over the railway), rivers leaving the map run on, the sea stays open (far islands only past 5.6 km, beyond where ships leave), mountain ranges along the horizon (taller in the Arctic and Iceland, snow by climate); its `extra` is exactly zero on the map and along its edge. The far ground is drawn in LOD rings (8 m at the seam, then 16 to 256 m out to 12 km, four quadrant meshes, ~137k triangles, was 320k to 5.3 km) whose first row of vertices is the landscape tiles' last (same height, normal and colour), coarse rings stitched to fine ones with no T-junctions; past 12 km the ridges are drawn as hazy silhouettes in the fog's colour out to 20 km; the far water in rings (40 to 640 m) meets the fine sheet, Arctic sea ice carried on. Neighbouring towns (`horizon::towns`): one where each group of ways off the map leads (highway and trunk each way, any road drawn off the map) plus 2-3 from the seed, on dry level far land 6-12 km out: towers and blocks as skylines in the haze by day, window bands, street lights, red roof lamps and a soft glow at night; the highway and railway run on past `WAY_REACH` to them (`horizon::ways`), the highway a string of lamps at night; shipping lanes run out to sea from each port and ferry terminal (or the sea's way out) with red/green buoy lights. Render only: nothing simulated, no sim change (one visibility change: `Landscape::carve` is pub). Rebuilt only when the map, the ways off it, the ports or the seam change (~0.3 s); the far ground no longer casts shadows (the old one sat in every shadow cascade). Medium quality, vsync off, 7 presets x high/low x day/night, same views: 215 fps before, 262 after. Tests `horizon::tests` (seam exact, deterministic per seed, coast/sea run on, far islands clear of ships' way out, triangle budget and no NaN per climate, towns where the ways lead), `rings::tests` (doubling rings meet on grid lines, watertight). Screenshots O:/nd_targets/r4-horizon/shots_base and shots_new (+ extra/). Left: towns are placeholders for step 3's real places (no names yet, no trade); far ribbons are draped, not graded; the flat map's far ground is the old hills plus ranges (no water); towns and lights are hidden in walk view by design (fog); for step 2, chunks of new land can call `FarLand::natural`/`water_distance` and a `rings::layout`-style grid per chunk.
  2. **Expandable map** (2–4 days): unlock land next to the city (tiles, see below); terrain is generated chunk by chunk from seed + coordinates; positions measured from a local origin that moves with the camera (f32 precision far from the centre); saves store only what changed.
  3. **Planet** (4–6 days): a world generator (continents, mountains, rivers, climate bands by latitude and altitude); the presets (coast, delta, arctic, tropical, islands...) become places on the globe; new game = pick a starting spot; other towns on the globe are the trade partners: highways, trains and ships leave towards real places in real directions (ferries already "stand at the far pier out of sight"; that pier becomes a real town).
  4. **Regions and sister cities** (4–6 days): opening a far region makes a new city or a district/sister city; regions not being looked at tick in summary (SimCity 4 style).
  5. **Later:** globe and space camera (city lights on the night side, satellites, launches, a spaceport); the optional **colony hard mode** (an empty planet, off-world trade by orbital shuttle from a starting launch site).
  - Tiles vs regions (explained to Harvey 2026-10-28; recommended both): tiles grow one continuous city (one simulation, roads run straight across, limited by how big one simulated city can get, ~50k people today); regions are separate cities of their own on the planet, joined by highways, rail and sea lanes, the one you are in simulated in full and the rest in summary.

- [x] Road tool: a ramp between two existing roads of different heights says "too steep" unless its level is raised first (Harvey 2026-10-28). Drawing a two-point road from a raised road to a ground road (no points between), the start already sits at the raised road's height and the end at the ground road's: those clicked ends should take the height of the road they snap to, whatever the Level dial says, and the check should judge the slope of the ramp that will actually be built between them (length vs height difference, the ramp's own grade limit). The Level dial should only set the height of points clicked on open ground (between the roads). Today the snapped end seems to take the dial's level (`ToolState::point_levels` filled from `elevation`, nd_app tools/state.rs ~384, tools/road.rs), so the grade check sees a jump to the raised road and refuses. Also check the hologram, the pillar planner and the implement path agree, and say in the warning how long the ramp must be for the height it climbs. → DONE [r4-ramp]: confirmed on a flat test city through the remote API (a 12 m viaduct, streets at grade 200 m and 80 m from it): the network always lays a ramp's end on the road it is dropped on at that road's height (`RoadNetwork::as_built`, used by the columns and implementing), but the tool put a clicked end at the Level dial's level unless the road was within 4.5 m of the dial. With the dial at the ground the 80 m ramp was warned "too steep" (rightly: 12 m in 80 m is 15%); with the dial raised to 12 m the same end sat at 12 m in the check, so the warning and the red went away while the hologram's deck still ran down to the street and the same 15% ramp was built. Now a point that is the end (first or last; while drawing, the newest point) snaps onto any road in reach, the one at about the dial's level first, and takes that road's height there (a node, or a point along a raised road's ramp between nodes, or a tunnel); once another point follows it is put again as a point between, which snaps only onto a road at about its own level (a road drawn under a viaduct passes under it); the dial sets only points on open ground (`RoadNetwork::snap_point_level`, `ToolState::click_road_point`/`road_with_cursor`/`PointClick`; `[`/`]` leave points on roads at their road's height). The hologram, the grade warnings and the column plan judge the road as built (`as_built` in `hologram` and `road_grade_warnings`), the remote `tool {draw}` puts points as clicks do and `add_road` snaps an end whatever level it was given. The warning says how long the ramp must be: "too steep between points 1 and 2: 15% over 80 m (at most 7%): a ramp needs 171 m for 12 m at 7%". Tests `world/ramp_draw_tests.rs` (snap at level / any, warnings as built, the ramp built joined at both heights with no column gap), `tools/ramp_tests.rs` (both dials, up onto a deck and onto the middle of a deck's ramp, a point between, both ends raised at 12 m and 6 m, a portal into a tunnel, `[`/`]`), `remote::dispatch` `a_ramp_drawn_through_the_api_takes_the_roads_heights`. Left: on hilly land the check uses the ground under each point plus the level, as before (a deck eased along the hills may stand a little off that)

- [x] "Demand for new buildings" as a floating window (Harvey 2026-10-28: "i want to make "Demand for new buildings" a part of the main pane a popup window we can move around"). Today it sits deep in City Data, Market, Businesses (nd_app ui/detail.rs `market_businesses`, the "Demand for new buildings" heading with `demand_bar` per land use and the "include planned / under construction" checkbox). Make it a small movable egui window (like SimCity's RCI meter) opened from a button on the main top bar, so it can stay open beside the city while building: the same bars, hover reasons and checkbox; position and open/closed state remembered between sessions; keep the section in City Data too, with a "pop out" button. Keep it compact enough not to cover the build pane. → DONE [r4-leisure]: a **Demand** button on the top bar (by City Data) opens a small movable window (`Info::Demand`, `ui/demand.rs`, 252 px wide, top right of the map by default, clear of the Build pane): one bar per zone (residential, commercial, industrial, civic, park) with the hover reasons and the "include planned / under construction" extension; commercial is the highest of its sub-zones with a row of small bars under it (neighbourhood, retail, offices, leisure; hover: what each grows). Its place, open state and the checkbox are saved in `settings.ron` (`DemandPrefs`, saved when changed with the mouse up) and restored next session. City Data, Market, Businesses keeps the same panel (hints under the bars, the chart) with a "Pop out" button. Remote `window {name: demand}`. Test: `the_window_state_is_saved_and_old_settings_load_without_it`.

- [x] Check: "Builders can't reach this plot" on every site of a day-15 save (r4-oldstone report 2026-10-28). The Old Stone agent loaded Harvey's newest day-15 save of his city (copied to its own ND_HOME) and nothing was built anywhere, with that reason on every plot, on main as well; it used the day-9 save instead and did not dig. The crossings agent's day-16 copy did grow, so it may be the agent's setup (save, version or load path) rather than the game. Find which save, reproduce on main, and if real find why builders' reach fails city-wide (and whether the crossings road relay on load changes it). → Not reproduced (2026-10-28): r4-glass ran his newest autosave and r4-patchwork his day-16 save, both build normally; the only "can't reach" plots were ones an agent zoned on a street it had not joined to the network. Likely the Old Stone agent's setup.

- [x ] Metro riders loop: re-plan on the platform, walk the viaduct and come back (Harvey 2026-10-28, his game day 46, Ezra Varga 8075:1 at Steam Alley 1000:1). Ezra walks to Steam Alley, goes down to the platform (-539,93) to wait (`trips/arrive.rs`, ToStop → `Activity::Waiting` at the platform), and a minute later sets off again for work by metro from the platform: a 42 min walk that comes back to the same platform, five times from 10:24 to 13:17 (`inspect {citizen, history: true}`). Causes: (1) `Activity::is_traveling` (citizens.rs ~199) leaves out `Waiting`, so `trips/decide.rs` ~65 lets the mind decide again for someone waiting at a stop once their `next_decision_tick` is due (it was set 20–60 min after setting off, so a long walk to the stop means it is already due on arrival): the decision re-plans the same trip from where they stand; (2) that re-plan starts from the platform point, underground, not the entrance (the give-up and line-closed paths move them up to `building_entrance` first, this one does not), and the platform sits under the Copper Circus viaduct, so the walk snaps onto the viaduct's walkway above (Kessler Drive viaduct): Harvey sees them "jumping up and down on an elevated road" he never joined a path to. Fix: a waiting transit rider is not re-decided (treat Waiting with a transit leg as travelling, the WAIT_GIVE_UP rule stays the way out); any re-plan from a platform goes up to the entrance first; a walk that starts at a point on the ground (or below) never snaps to a raised way's walkway. Check bus stops too (a waiting bus rider re-decided resets `since`, so WAIT_GIVE_UP may never fire). Measure how many riders loop like this on his save before/after. → DONE [r4-metroloop]: both causes confirmed on a copy of his day-46 save (`examples/metro_loop`). (1) A rider waiting at a stop, platform, pad or kerb with a trip under way (`Citizen::waiting_for_ride`) is no longer decided for, and a late LLM answer for them is kept for later like one for someone on the train (`apply_late_decision`); with no more re-plans the wait's start stays put, so `WAIT_GIVE_UP` can fire. What decisions did for them before at night is now the give-up at the end of service: once the last bus/metro has had `LAST_RUN_GRACE` (30 min, trains 60) to come, whoever still waits goes another way ("Missed the last train", no bad-commute memory; `transit::service_over`). (2) Any way off a platform comes up to the station entrance first (`up_from_platform`, in `travel_with`, `leave_building`, `wander`). (3) Snapping: the platform under Steam Alley lies 8 m from the Kessler Drive viaduct's pavement, nearer than any on the ground, so `WalkGraph::route` started every walk up there; walk nodes along a deck (≥ `VIADUCT_LEVEL`) are now `WalkNode::deck` and a route end that is out from under the deck's footprint snaps to the nearest node on the ground (`snap_end`; a point at a node or on the deck, a bus stop up there, still snaps onto it). Also the drawing: `way_height_on_leg` lifted any leg running alongside a raised way within reach (the station lot's loop under the viaduct's ramp was drawn on the ramp); a leg between walk nodes not laid along a raised way (`WalkNode::lifted`) is now at grade. Same save, 26 h from midnight, main → branch: metro boardings 3,125 → 4,699, bus 528 → 1,316; riders who left the wait without boarding: metro 6,248 → 1,081 (left are those who missed the last train after midnight), bus 1,272 → 135; set off again from a platform point 6,213 → 0; back at a stop they had left the same trip: metro 3,284 / bus 316 → 0 / 4 (2,151 people → 4); door-to-door home→work mean 152.8 → 146.7 min (median 112 → 108, p90 360 → 330); failed trips 520 → 510. Day-36 save: metro boardings 3,280 → 4,321, bus 602 → 1,330, loops 1,896 metro / 483 bus → 0 / 1, commute 166.2 → 163.1 min. Tests `a_waiting_metro_rider_whose_decision_is_due_waits_for_the_train`, `a_trip_planned_again_from_the_platform_starts_at_the_entrance`, `riders_still_waiting_after_the_last_train_go_another_way`, `a_walk_from_beside_a_viaduct_does_not_climb_onto_it`, `a_walk_along_the_ground_beside_a_viaduct_stays_on_the_ground`. Left: people still plan a metro trip at 23:xx whose walk reaches the platform after the last train (~300 a night come up again); ground directly under a deck's footprint is still ambiguous in 2D (a walk from there may start on the deck, as before)

- [ ] Commutes are very long; late riders pick a metro that has stopped (r4-metroloop report 2026-10-28). On Harvey's day-46 autosave the home-to-work trip averages 147 min (median 108, p90 330) even after the metro loop fix (day 36: 163 min), and Ezra Varga's memories say "152 minutes in the saddle. Too far to ride." Find where the time goes (walks to far stations, 30-min headway on the one loop line, bike trips across town, waits), and whether job and home choice should weigh the commute more. Also about 300 people a night choose the metro around 23:xx and reach the platform after the last train: mode choice should check the ride starts before service ends (`transit::service_over`). → PARTLY DONE [r4-timetable]: the late riders: mode choice now asks the line's timetable whether a train still calls at their station (within a headway) by the time they get down to the platform, and buses whether one is still out a headway after they reach the stop; riders waiting for the first train of the morning are no longer told it is over. Missed the last bus or train on the save: 598 a day (metro 691+224+61+30 over two days at 00–03h) → 80 a day. A 24-hour metro (Line 1 every 30 min all night) adds about 1250 night boardings a day for 50 a day in runs and leaves nobody stranded. Left: where the commute time goes (mean 147 min, median 109, p90 334 on main and branch alike: the Red Line's loop takes about 220 min round, so even 10 buses run 22 min apart; walks to far stations, the 30-min metro), and whether job and home choice should weigh the commute.

- [x] Transit timetables: service hours, night service and rush-hour frequency per line (Harvey 2026-10-28: "we dont have overnight trains? we should whats the schedule like? can we set hours and night/day/rush-hour schedule?"). Today every mode has fixed hours in code and one frequency all day: buses 05:30–23:30 (`transit::SERVICE_START/END`), metro 05:00–24:00 (`subway.rs`), trains 05:00–23:00 (`rail/mod.rs`), ferries 06:00–22:00 (`ports.rs`), air taxis 05:00–02:00 (`air.rs`); metro and rail lines have one `headway` (Harvey's Line 1 runs every 30 min), bus lines a fixed number of buses. Nothing changes at rush hour and nothing runs through the night. Add a timetable per line (bus, metro, rail, ferry): four day parts (morning rush ~06–09, day, evening rush ~16–19, evening) plus an optional night service (24-hour line, or a sparse night headway), each with its own headway (buses: buses out; extra buses leave and return to the depot as the part changes), presets ("commuter", "all day", "24 hours", "night owl") and a city default; cost follows the runs actually made (upkeep per run, so night service costs money); rush-hour crowding and riders' waits shown per part in the Lines window; mode choice and the end-of-service give-up (`transit::service_over`, r4-metroloop) read the line's real hours, so nobody picks a line that has stopped (fixes the ~300 people a night reaching the platform after the last train). Old saves: lines get today's hours and headway as their timetable. Remote: `bus/subway/rail ... timetable`. → DONE [r4-timetable]: every bus line, metro line, the passenger trains and each ferry terminal has a timetable (`nd_sim::timetable`): service hours, a morning and an evening rush, the day and the evening, and an optional night service (off / sparse / 24 hours), each part with its headway or buses out (extra buses leave and, once a rush ends, take nobody on, let their riders off and go home), presets Commuter / All day / 24 hours / Night owl and a city default for new lines ("apply to every line" too). A day timeline per line (boarded per hour today vs yesterday, riders waiting and on board, runs, riders left after the last run), shaded by the timetable's parts, in the Lines window under every open line (part edges drag on the chart, a dial per part, average wait per part, runs and cost a day) and in City Data, Transit ("A day on each line"); trains and ferries have their own rows in the Lines window now. Costs follow the runs (buses per bus-minute, the metro per run; trains 60 and ferries 40 a run beyond the standard timetable). Riders read the line's real timetable: no line that has stopped, or (metro) no train still due at their station within a headway when they reach the platform; the end-of-service give-up uses the line's own hours. Old saves keep today's hours and frequency (no timetable = the standard). Remote: `timetable {list|get|set|preset|default}` (docs/REMOTE.md). On Harvey's day-46 save, 2 days: defaults as main within noise (bus 1200 vs 1247 boardings/day, wait 34.0 vs 34.4 min; metro 4935 vs 4827, wait 15.4 both), riders missing the last bus/train 598 → 80 a day. Left: air taxis keep their fixed hours; Commuter on the buses there needs 24 buses at the rush against 22 depot places and its thinner evening made bus waits longer (34 → 40 min): build a depot first; ferries' waits are not timed.

- [ ] Footbridge stairs: spiral or switchback stairs where a straight flight would be too long or steep (Harvey 2026-10-28: "we should have spiral foot-bridges or back/forth ones for steep stairs if we can"). Today a footbridge's stairs run straight down each pavement; where the pavement is short, the deck high or the street busy, use a switchback (back-and-forth flights with landings) or a spiral stair in a tower, chosen by the room the pavement has; the walk graph follows the stair's real path (length and climb), cyclists wheel their bikes on them (r4-crossings `World::wheeling_bike`). Also check Harvey's report that riders "were literally flying, not using the footbridge at all visually": he saw it on the pre-crossings-fix exe (neon_district.brick.exe); confirm on the current build that cyclists are drawn walking the bike up the stairs and over the deck, and that no rider is drawn climbing in mid-air beside a footbridge or a raised path.

- [x] Hillside city: build streets, paths and lots on mountains like Hong Kong or San Francisco (Harvey 2026-10-28: "trying to make a path on a mountain, it cut up the nice hill and made the path stick out strangely ... work on building on strange topology. i want like hongkong or sanfrancisco. can we zone/build streets on moutainious regious?"; earlier flag F15: "we need all our buildings and structures to handle strange hills, like mexico city or hongkong or san francisco (the sidewalks literally become steps sometimes)"). His flag: target/release/flags/claude_flags.jsonl line 135 (day 3 16:11, path 1216 "Unnamed path" from (1426.6,53.3) to (1399.4,25.6), run of 6 pieces 420 m, at grade, on a mountain island: "it just created a really weird stairs with weird land transforms"); line 85 (the older "levelled the hill and put the path on stilts") was fixed in r1-roads (K5), so check whether line 135 is a new case of the same (paths sampled densely but the ground still regraded round them, steps drawn oddly) on a hilly island save (find which save has a day-3 mountain island city with path 1216; else make one with `new_game {preset: islands, relief: 1}`). Today: streets keep a 7% grade (`world/water/mod.rs` STREET_GRADE), so on a hillside a street is eased onto cuttings and embankments that carve the hill; paths may go 60% (stairs past 12%); lots grade up to slope 0.7 and hillsides up to 1.8 / 40 m relief take terraced homes, shops and clubs (`LotGround::Hillside`, fix/pt-parcels). Make: (1) paths and stairs that lie on the hill with minimal earthwork (no "land transforms" beyond a narrow bench; flights and landings drawn on the real slope); (2) steep local streets up to ~25–30% (San Francisco), avenues ~10–12%, highways and rail as today, with cars and buses slowing on climbs and the tool warning past each kind's limit; streets that follow the land rather than a flattened ramp (the earthwork band only as wide as the street, retaining walls where it cuts); switchback streets made easy (the road tool offering a zig-zag on a slope); stair streets (a street whose pavements become steps, cars barred past a grade); (3) buildings on slopes: stepped floors down the hill, podiums on stilts over the drop and entrances on the street side at street level (Hong Kong Mid-Levels), retaining walls and terraces between lots, cliffside mansions; (4) zoning: say why a hillside won't zone and how steep is allowed; (5) check walkers, the walk graph and parking follow the new grades. Measure on a hilly preset (islands, relief 1) and on his mountain island: how much ground each street and path moves before and after, screenshots close and high. → DONE [r4-hillside]: line 135 was the same case as line 85 in a new place: the path lay on the land, but the ground round it was graded with 1-in-2.5 side slopes, and on a mountainside steeper than that a slope off any held cell never met the land again, so the hill was regraded into cones and spikes tens of metres out. Now (1) a street or path across a hillside (cross-fall 25%+, or 1.5 m into or above the land) stands in a narrow bench held by walls (`Hold::Bench`, band a wall's thickness past the verge), the walls drawn per side where the land needs them, with the hillside drawn back over the terrain mesh's coarse cells; side slopes anywhere follow steep land (land + 15%, to 1 in 1.5) instead of 1 in 2.5; stairs follow the real slope in even flights (risers every 17 cm of climb, carried over gentle stretches, handrails on long flights) instead of leg by leg. (2) Local streets climb to 25%, avenues to 11% (`land_grade`), highways, slip roads, ramps onto decks and rail as before; cars slow past 12% (about 60% of the limit up 25%), buses and lorries past 8% (about 50%), all brake down past 12%, units under siren keep their speed; the Road tool warns where the land climbs past the kind's limit and offers a zig-zag (X, or the pane's button; `add_road` replies `switchback`). (3) Lots on a steep hillside are walled terraces: ground held at the pad, podium walls over the drop (piers and dark bays past 3.5 m) and walls holding the hill back, the land beyond left alone. (4) The Zone tool hover, `paint` refusals (`ground`) and parcel inspect (`ground_note`) say how steep the plot is and the limits (graded 70% / 18 m, terraced 180% / 40 m). Measured (`examples/hillside_probe.rs`): Harvey's island (day-2 save), his path rebuilt along 394 m ending on his flagged piece moved 76,900 m³ (band 35 m, worst 18.7 m) before, 4,050 m³ (band 8.7 m, worst 2.3 m) after; a street up the mountain 377,000 m³ (band 47 m, cuts to 49 m) before, 51,800 m³ (band 9.6 m, cuts to 22 m where it climbs 54% land at 25%, warned) after; islands relief 1 test sites (seeds 7 and 11): streets 69-79 → 16-17 m³/m, avenues 68-101 → 36-40 m³/m, paths unchanged (they lay on gentler sites); his day-9 city as built: streets 13.3 → 10.2 m³/m, avenues 17.5 → 13.1. Demo district over 8 seeds: days 18-20 unemployment 10.3% → 9.7% average.
  - [ ] Hillside leftovers (r4-hillside): stepped floors down the hill inside a building and entrances placed on the street side at street level (buildings still stand on the levelled pad; the podium walls stand in for the Mid-Levels look); stair streets as their own kind (today the warning points to a path, which already climbs in steps and fronts homes; a street whose pavements turn to steps with cars barred is not built); the walk graph does not yet slow walkers on stairs and climbs (walkers are drawn on the ground and steps, but walk times are by length); the zig-zag offer is a plain one-leg check and is not offered where the land between is too uneven for every leg to keep the grade; the terrain mesh's 8 m cells still show faceted shading beside benches; cliffside mansions as a style for the steepest terraced lots; parking on terraced lots and walkers on benches not checked in a running city (walkers are set on the built ground, which the benches and terraces hold at the way's and the pad's height).

- [ ] Sound, synthesised (Harvey 2026-10-28: "we can start with simple sinthesized as you planned then work through them"). The game has no audio yet. The plan agreed: the city is a mix, not one sound per car or person. Layers: (1) ambience beds by camera height (high: distant city wash and wind; mid: traffic roar; street: close bed), crossfaded by zoom and driven by what is under the camera (cars, walkers, district look, time of day, weather); (2) a few spot sounds near the camera (trains, metro rumble, sirens, construction, clubs at night, horns in angry jams, rain and thunder), hard voice cap ~16–24, loudest win, distance falloff; (3) UI sounds (road drawn, building placed, bulldoze, window open, errors), quiet and short; (4) optional generative music (later). Mixer sliders (master, ambience, effects, UI, music) in Settings, saved; mute when the window loses focus. All synthesised in code (no samples): render-side only, never touches the sim or determinism. Phases: 1 engine + mixer + UI sounds + the three beds; 2 spot sounds from the sim; 3 music.
  - [x] Phase 1 (engine, mixer, UI sounds, three beds) → DONE [r4-sound]: new crate `nd_audio` (cpal 0.17.3 only, Apache-2.0, already in the tree via Bevy; Bevy's own idle AudioPlugin disabled): own DSP on cpal's callback thread fed by a channel, telemetry back by atomics; silent with no device or `ND_AUDIO=0`. Buses master/ambience/effects/UI/music (music silent), squared slider curve, peak limiter + soft clip at -0.2 dBFS, DC blocker. Settings window SOUND section (5 sliders, mute, out of focus: mute/turn down/keep), saved in settings.ron (serde defaults). 16 UI cues found by watching state (tool, road points, project steps, implement, buildings, refusals, speed/pause, windows, City Data, selection, toasts, egui clicks), rate limited (gap per cue, softer repeats, 10/s, a click gives way to the cue it caused). Beds by camera height (street <30 m, mid 70–220 m, high >650 m, equal-power crossfade), sampled 4×/s: moving cars and speed, walkers, density, neon/night market, parks/suburbs (birds by day, crickets at night), water lap, rain hiss and drops, wind from the weather, snow and the Underground view muffle. Remote `audio {get,set,play}`, `audio_capture`; `cargo run -p nd_audio --example render_wavs -- <dir>` renders every cue and bed. Audio thread ~1% of its time budget; fps on/off equal within noise
  - [ ] Phase 2: spot sounds from the sim (trains, metro, sirens, construction, clubs, horns in jams, thunder): engine has `SPOT_VOICES` cap, `VoicePool` (quietest stolen) and `distance_gain` ready, effects bus wired
  - [ ] Phase 3: generative music (music bus and slider in place)

## Follow-ups noted in agent reports (not started)
- [x] Harvey's day-62 city empties through hunger (found by p1-sim-leftovers while checking G2's population): run on from his day-62 save, every version (before G2, with G2, with G2's growth switched off) loses ~5,000 people in a week to "Couldn't keep myself fed here" (`LeaveReason::Hunger`, 4 hungry days), 600–900 a day, while 2,200–3,300 are hungry at any time and the grocers open climb 22 → 57; population 16,660 → ~15,000 by day 68. Find why people there cannot get fed (grocers' stock and deliveries, meals, distance, money) before judging growth changes on that save. → DONE [r4-hunger]: not food: grocers had 9–13k groceries on the shelves and only ~300 of 8,600 pantries were empty. Four holds, found with `examples/hunger_week` (day by day, with a 20:00 breakdown of the hungry) and `examples/stuck_riders` (`WALK=1` for walkers): (1) 2,220 cyclists stood ~20 h at one T (node 1461, (583,123)) coming in on a one-way street out of a signalised junction: no lane comes in on that arm, no phase serves it, so the rider's "green" never came (`approach_unlit`; they now go in a gap in the cross traffic, as with no lights); (2) ~100 riders waited all day at a mid-block zebra in front of a busy grocer that never emptied (they now wheel through after `ZEBRA_PATIENCE` 5 min); (3) with those moving, ~900 walkers stood a day and more at two unsignalised junction crossings ((584,41), (752,93)) holding back for drivers stuck in a jam (`driver_waiting`): after `JAM_WALK_THROUGH` 4 min they cross between the standing cars (moving cars still count for the gap); (4) 1,100 of the remaining hungry were people with no home and no camp bed (2,400 unhoused, half without a bed; the mission van feeds only those with one): they ate only at home, so starved with food in the pantry and money; a household with no home now eats its groceries where it is (`sleep_and_eat`) and its hungry grown-ups buy groceries when the bag is empty (nd_minds: they only ever wandered looking for a bunk). Same save, 7 days from midnight (main → this branch): population 16,660 → 14,979 vs → 18,434; left over hunger 405/501/817/811/820/793 a day (4,147) → 14/12/72/93/78/106 (375); hungry at 23:59 3,145–4,134 → 450–1,348 (hourly mean 2,650–3,694 → 496–1,161); grocers 22 → 50 vs 22 → 55, shelf stock 9.5–18.5k vs 4.4–8k (selling it: meals sold 21–27k → 31.8–36.6k a day, grocery imports 0 → 4.7–11.6k a day as the town grows). Left: the remaining hungry are mostly drivers and riders on very slow trips (resident cars average ~2 m/s, trips of many hours, the known slow-traffic issue) and broke people on the street; a few riders still wait long at junction 943 ((-551,-150)) whose phase sat on [0,1] for 100 min. demo_sweep 1 16, main → branch: day 8 15.9% → 15.7%, days 18–20 8.1% → 6.8%, closed 27.4 → 26.5, pop 3,800 → 3,849. Tests `a_cyclist_coming_in_against_a_one_way_is_not_held_for_good`, `walkers_held_back_for_a_driver_stuck_in_a_jam_walk_through`, `a_household_with_no_home_eats_its_groceries_where_it_is`, `the_homeless_buy_food_when_the_bag_is_empty`

## Next steps (Harvey 2026-10-08) — ON HOLD: Harvey said pause after the code review; start these another day only when he asks
- [→] City rating: liveability, prosperity, safety, environment — measured, with history charts (no challenges/scenarios yet)
- [→] Banks and debt: bank businesses open and lend to other businesses (loans can go bad, defaults, failures like real life); broke people can fall into debt (hospital bills, gambling, etc.) with consequences
- [→] Large ferry terminal + car ferries: a bigger terminal (vehicle ramps, marshalling lanes, needs a road connection) whose ferries carry cars and trucks as well as passengers; routing treats car-ferry links as part of the road network (with a fare and a wait), so drivers and freight can cross water
- [→] Regional commuters: while local labour is short, businesses hire out-of-town workers who drive in on the highway (dearer wage, travel time, sometimes late, highway rush hours); as residents arrive locals are cheaper/closer and are preferred, so the commuter share falls; City Data shows "jobs held by commuters %" as a goal to drive to 0; residents without local work commute out (wages spent elsewhere)
- [→] New business kinds (append to enums, each with its own look, demand and staffing):
  - construction firms (builders who actually put buildings up and employ crews)
  - pharmacy, private clinics, doctors, dentists; childcare/daycare
  - clothing, hardware, furniture shops; fuel/EV charging stations, car wash
  - hair salons/barbers, gyms, laundromats; trades (plumbers, electricians, repair shops)
  - food variety: fast food, pizza place, noodle bar, restaurants; nightlife split: clubs, cinemas, theatres
  - office economy: software studios, data centres (big power, few jobs), call centres, media, security, cleaning, real estate, law, insurance
  - recycling plants (ties into trash and contamination)
- [→] Cable cars (on hilly maps)
- [→] Monorail: a new elevated train type running above the road, hanging (suspended) design like Wuppertal / Shonan; stations need no zoning but must connect to a road or path at their connection points to work
- [→] Transit economics: ridership per line, fare/fee settings per line and info
- [→] Performance with big cities (20k–50k people): profile and fix — a must
- [→] Faster save and load (and smaller saves); history autosaves keep 24 × ~150 MB per big city (~3.6 GB) — fewer or compressed
- [→] Settings menu (graphics, controls, UI scale, etc.)
- (Later / skipped for now: challenges and scenarios, elections and disasters, water and sewage, seasons and events, advisor and heat maps, neighbouring towns, sound, tutorial)
- [x] Level crossings: a crossing signal bar that comes down and stops traffic when a train passes (on streets of more than 2 lanes), alternating red flashing lights, a pole going down and up → DONE [feat/rail-crossing]: each crossing runs a cycle from the trains coming (lights 15 s out, arms down 3 s later over 5 s, down while a train is over it and 2 s after, up over 5 s; saved with serde defaults); vehicles stop at a stop line clear of the track (lanes end there), walkers and cyclists at the track's edge; trains wait for a crossing not closed with its box clear; half-barriers + pavement gates on 3+ lane streets, lights/bell/crossbuck only on 1+1, lights and gates on footpaths; posts with twin lamps flashing in turn, crossbucks, bells, striped arms with tip lamps swinging smoothly; road inspector section; `level_crossings` API (and in `intersection`)

## Playtest round 2026-10-10 — everything open, by category
Sources: docs/playtest/todo_2026-10-10.md (Harvey's list, "T"), docs/playtest/flags_2026-10-09.md (in-game flags F1–F44), and the open items above.

### A. Traffic and vehicles [fix/pt-traffic]
- [x] Cars appear/vanish: a line of "on the road" cars with no driver drive to one spot and vanish (F44, T) — cars must always exist somewhere or leave town   → DONE [fix/pt-traffic]: they were sold cars (Errand::Sale) all set off at the daily household tick to the one dealer and were deleted at its kerb. A sold car now belongs to the dealer (`OwnedCar::dealer`), waits where it was parked until the dealer's driver fetches it in working hours (3/hour/dealer, each car at its own hour), parks on the forecourt as used stock (drawn in rows inside the lot), is resold to the next buyer or goes to the trade out of town after 3 days (driven off the map); deliveries leave the forecourt 4/hour; labels say "a used suv being sold" with the dealer's driver at the wheel
- [x] Lane choice: drivers in a left-turn-only lane wanting to turn right; 90° lane changes at the stop line block the junction (F20, F21)   → DONE [fix/pt-traffic]: reproduced in Harvey's save at (-361,314): a 34 m piece past a bend, left lane L-only; queue costs made the planner put the change into the last 2 m (cheaper than joining the right lane's queue upstream). Changes with < 12 m before the solid line now cost 1000 m, late changes pay per car on the lane they cut into, and cars whose route uses a turn the adapted arrows no longer allow replan
- [x] Raging driver loops through tunnels for 1,431 min on a simple trip (F18); raging drivers may make illegal U-turns by personality (T)   → DONE [fix/pt-traffic]: the stalled-reroute round every full exit took any way at all (and the F20 junction kept sending it round); a way round a full exit is now taken only within +350 m / +60% of the drive left, else it waits; drivers with anger ≥ 0.6 may turn round mid-block on a two-way street when the other way is ≥150 m quicker (queues counted), with odds from (1 − compliance) × anger (`rage_u_turns`, `Driving::rage_u_turns_*`)
- [x] Vehicle build-up past ~10k people (grid_plaza seed 3, Perfect AI City day 20+): vehicles grow 300–500/day at flat population   → DONE [fix/pt-traffic]: census (examples/vehicle_census) of the day-25 Perfect AI City: 1,273 of 2,544 vehicles were cars being sold and 620 deliveries (555 queued at one dealer's kerb): jams made people sell, failing transit made them buy, every trade two more cars into the jam. Trades now hold 14 days (`Commute::wheels_day`), dealer traffic is spread over the day and capped, and old backlogs drain (stuck deliveries back to the forecourt, overdue sales pull in and go to the trade). Same save: 2,544 → ~1,250 vehicles by day 30 instead of growing
- [x] Parking: driver "looking for parking" passes three lots with room (F5); lot shows 13/30 with 3 cars visible (F35)   → DONE [fix/pt-traffic]: F5: hunters only weighed lots within 180 m of the door; now a hunter about to pass an open lot's gate with room (within 360 m of the destination) turns in (`hunters_spot_lots`), and desperate hunters look at lots twice as far. F35: the drawn layout fitted a fixed grid with a 3 m margin (a 1,039 m² parcel got 9 bays); `nd_sim::parking_layout` packs bays (both row directions, offsets) and a surface lot's spaces are its bays, drawn and counted alike (old saves re-counted on load)
- [x] Tow trucks visibly tow (car hooked behind), tow-truck look (F34); big tow trucks for RVs   → DONE [fix/pt-traffic]: new bodies TowTruck (cab, boom and hook, wheel lift, amber bar) and HeavyTow (three axles, rotator boom) replace the pickup; the towed car is drawn with its own body on the wheel lift, nose up, trailing round corners; lorries, buses and RV-sized bodies get the heavy one (`BodyType::needs_heavy_tow`). Left: RVs themselves are homeless households' `Rv` records, not vehicles, so nothing tows them yet (needs RVs as vehicles, F/G)

### B. Transit sim [fix/pt-transit]
- [x] Buses never leave depots; lines say "no buses sent" (F3, F4); do lines need to touch a depot? explain + fix → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: lines need not touch a depot. The dispatcher only ever tried the first line short of buses, and when that one's bus could not be sent it retried forever and every later line starved (Harvey's day-67 save: "Full City" and "Line 6" never had a bus, with 30 bays free). Now every line is served on its own, from the nearest depot with room that can reach it; `World::line_problems` says why not (no depot, none reaches stop 1, depots full, fleet short, night 23:30-05:30). Also: a leg running back along its own street was "no road" (now routed round the block).
- [x] Bus leaves its route / wrong turn, 568 min trip (F6, F7); routes validated while editing (show unreachable legs before Finish) → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: buses drive each leg's drawn lanes, wait for a lane change instead of missing the turn, detour only after 4 min blocked; each leg is timed from its last stop (568 min was the whole day since the depot). F6's leg was itself a 585 m drive for stops 90 m apart: flagged as `long_way_round`. `Transit::check_stops` / `World::line_problems` feed the Lines window (C's adapter now maps onto them); remote `bus problems|check`.
- [x] Buses bunch: release from depot on a schedule (F36); warn when not enough buses/depots for the lines (T) → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: buses leave a headway apart (loop / buses, at most 20 min), join the loop at stops spread round it, and are held up to 60 s at a stop reached under half a headway after the last; `fleet_short` problem and `bus_fleet_short` / `bus_line_gets_no_buses` alerts.
- [x] A single passenger station gets no riders — it must link to out of town like ferries (T) → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: rail visitors step off a calling train, out-of-town commuters come by morning train (while the town has under 3% unemployed), residents take day trips to the city down the line; all counted as station riders and fares (`world/rail_travel.rs`).
- [x] Metro riders wait at the kerb, should go down into the station (F23) → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: they go in and wait on the platform, hidden at street level and drawn on the platform in the Underground view; the inspector says "on the metro platform".
- [x] Explain and fix low bus/metro ridership vs lots of walking + traffic complaints (T) → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: write-up in docs/playtest/transit_ridership_2026-10-10.md (bikes win most 500 m commutes; two lines never had buses; stops out of reach; one-way loops; no transfers; every bus ride counted as painful). Fixed: transfers between lines, headway-based waits, stop reach 160 m, fairer transit pain, unservable lines not offered; `report {section: "ridership"}`.
- [x] Transit economics: ridership per line, fares per line (next steps) → DONE [fix/pt-transit]: per-line fare (Lines window, `bus set_fare`), books per line (riders, fares, upkeep yesterday); bus upkeep charged per line by bus-minutes run (it was charged on the buses out at midnight, i.e. almost none).

Amusement park, convention centre, cathedral, casino not done.
