Parks and services tuning left as it was.

## 2. Roads, intersections and the road tools
- [x] Intersections are one joint: dragging the junction dot moves every road attached; roads meeting at a point mend into one junction on Implement and can't be pulled apart (only by demolishing a leg) (T) → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: Edit tool grabs a junction (one gold dot) and drags every road meeting there as one undo step (a road running through gets a point there); Implement mends ends/through-roads onto one shared point (geometry-checked, nothing on the map moves)
- [x] Edit: click a road piece to edit its own end points (and disconnect it); otherwise dragging moves the whole junction (T) → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: a picked road's own points (orange) drag on their own; remote `tool {edit_drag}`
- [x] Road tool UI cleanup: pick road type → parameters (lanes, height; depth clearly shown as "tunnel" when deep enough) → draw method (straight, curved, circle); "highway ramp" is just a 1-lane one-way; special roads (interchange) as now (T) → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: types Path/Street/One-way/Highway + Special; lanes, level with a TUNNEL note and a tunnel button; Straight/Curved/Circle (closed smooth loop); old presets still work in the API (+ road_type, draw_method)
- [x] Crosswalk option "Remove crosswalk" (no crossing here) to shape pedestrian traffic; two crosswalks close together block a footbridge — allow turning them off (T, G3) → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: `CrossingUpgrade::Removed` in the Intersection editor (own row) and `intersection {upgrade: removed}`: no paint, no walk light, walkers route round, no jaywalk planned within 30 m; unmarked crossings (roundabout lanes, ramp mouths, dead ends) can now take a footbridge, button or removal (G3's circus crossing was refused as unmarked)
- [x] Footbridge didn't remove the crosswalk (G13); a visual crosswalk drawn in the middle of an intersection (G20) → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: a footbridge also replaces other crosswalks over its street within 30 m (G13's path zebra 27 m away); the painter matches closed crosswalks along the arm (a short/skewed arm's walk crossing sat >2 m from the painted zebra, so G20's 10 m Aurora Broadway stub kept its zebra in the island); no path zebra painted on a roundabout ring
- [x] Paths end at the junction centre instead of the pavement (G12); footbridge corners should look like one continuous deck (G14); missing inner pavement on a circus road (G9) → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: raised-path decks are drawn per run, mitred through corners with unbroken parapets (G14 was a raised path, short stubs no longer break the run) and stop at the back of the kerb where they come down onto a street (G12, visual; the snap of the path's end onto the street node is the road tool's, left to g2); footbridge rings drawn as one mitred walkway; circuses of radius >= 40 m get an inner pavement (G9). G12 snapping: [fix/roads-tools] the Road tool now drops a path end by a street on its pavement, clear of the junction node
- [x] Traffic signals added at an intersection but not visible (T) → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: switching signals changed no road or lane version, so heads and poles were never redrawn; now `signals_version` keys the redraw
- [x] Placing a road still re-adjusts plots/buildings all around it after Implement (the plan showed only the red buyouts) (T) — check fix/pt-parcels' local recut on Harvey's newer save → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: recut re-subdivided whole blocks while buildings were within 110 m; now a block with a building the works don't come over is recut only near the road and built parcels go only where the road overlaps them; the plan's red also shows every building the trial rebuild takes
- [x] Zoning civic round a circus levelled ground and made many parcels "unusable ground" (G5) → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: backland nothing in front could fold in became landlocked lots; now refused by the Zone tool, left unzoned (taken out of the stroke) if painted anyway, and kept empty lots are recut so new backland can join them
- [x] Uncommitted plan: show a prominent "Implement / Discard" bar at the top when the map has unimplemented changes (G2); planning "show" highlights the parcel(s) an action affects (T) → DONE [fix/roads-tools]: "NOT BUILT YET" bar (Implement, Discard asks twice, Show); committee Show outlines the parcels/ground (magenta) and buildings standing there (orange) for 45 s

## 3. Pedestrians and cyclists
- [x] People jaywalk instead of using footbridges, causing crashes and jams (G4, G7, G17, G20) — routes must prefer bridges; people already mid-route re-plan when a crossing becomes a footbridge; cyclists use footbridges too → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: causes: jaywalk edges (4 m penalty) stayed beside bridges; walkers with routes from before the bridge waited at the closed crosswalk for a gap and ran (G17); roundabout ring crossings were free unmarked short cuts (G7/G20 "braking for someone crossing (no crosswalk)"); crosswalks next to a bridge stayed. Fix: no jaywalk planned within 80 m of a bridge, jaywalks dearer per lane and none over 5+ lanes, jaywalkers give up after 5 ticks, ring crossings +60 m, walkers re-plan at a closed/shut crossing, cyclists wheel bikes over bridges (spliced path, walk pace). Harvey's save, same 4 h morning: unmarked at-grade crossings town-wide 1984 -> 672, footbridge uses 1017 -> 1460 (+638 bikes), G17 jaywalks 18 -> 0, G20 unmarked 327 -> 5 and its bridge 0 -> 61, G7 unmarked 72 -> 17 (bridge 0 -> 15 + 171 bikes); `walk_crossings` survey command
- [x] Walkers backing up a highway exit with no crosswalk to replace (G11) → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: slip-road mouths were unmarked crossings walkers used freely; now shut unless an off-ramp at a signalled junction (walk lights, marked), a push button or a footbridge (stairs down each street's pavement); never an on-ramp's mouth without one (G11's on-ramp mouth: 196 crossings in 4 h -> 0)
- [x] Hurt person lying in the road with nobody helping: an indicator and an ambulance (G4) → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: G4's "hurt" woman was homeless, sleeping rough where she stood (in the circus's lanes; no medical call). Now nobody idles in a street's lanes (moved to the pavement every 10 ticks) nor lies/sits there; the genuinely hurt (open road medical call, ambulance already dispatched by 911) get a pulsing red cross over them; `report` pedestrians `hurt_in_the_street_now`
- [x] Bike racks on buses (4 bikes): tired cyclists take the bus if there is room, else wait (T) → DONE [fix/walk-crossings]: a bike-and-bus option for owners facing a long ride (ride to the stop, rack, ride on); a bus boards bikes while its 4-place rack has room, the rest wait ("rack was full") for the next; `report` transit `bike_rack_rides_total`, `bike_rack_full_total`, `bikes_on_racks_now`

## 4. Vehicles and services
- [x] Tow trucks stuck on jobs blocking ramps and highways for hours (G10, G15, G19, G20)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: cause: after a U-turn or the one-way move a truck came back onto the lanes of the nearest "street" by 2D distance, often a slip road or highway passing beside/over it, from which no road led to its yard; it pulled in there to retry for ever. Now it rejoins the street the move was made in (else the nearest ordinary street, preferring a lane that reaches its goal); a truck with no road to its yard takes the car to the pound out of town; and a watchdog sends on (30 min) and finally clears (90 min) any service vehicle standing still on a carriageway for a reason of its own (queued in a jam does not count). Harvey's stuck trucks leave within the hour on his save.
- [x] Dev "Airlift" button: a Chinook lifts a stuck vehicle away (G19)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: "Airlift (4000)" in the inspector of any vehicle on the road (paid emergency service; not buses or units on a call); a tandem-rotor helicopter flies in from the map edge, hovers, slings it and flies off; a resident's car goes to the pound, a tow truck's job is settled. Remote `airlift {car}`.
- [x] Police cars look like police cars (black and white) even with lights off; "parked on the beat" while sitting in traffic (G8)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: black-and-white body (white doors and roof, push bar, gold shield) with the light bar's housing and red/blue lenses always on the roof; a patrol car on its beat now pulls in to the kerb (it was left standing in the lane where its route ended); on a highway or ramp it drives on instead.
- [x] Delivery trucks only in working hours (early start ok, rare night rush); not all at midnight (T)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: orders are queued (`World::freight_queue`) and each lorry leaves at its buyer's own minute: 05:00–07:30 first deliveries, nothing after 20:00, about 2.5% night rushes; within the day a restock round goes out over the hour, not in one minute.
- [x] Freight ships turn and berth sideways at the port; cranes move containers on/off by the ship's actual imports/exports (G6)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: freighters turn in their last 90 m to lie broadside across the pier head; the quay crane's trolley and spreader land one box per import load (by size) and then load the city's exports since the last call (hourly surplus, `Ports::outbound`), spread over the stay. Remote `ships` shows boxes and the crane's move.
- [x] Only one police HQ and one fire HQ per city (T)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: a second is refused in the preview and on placing: "the city has its police headquarters already (name): only one per city; build a police station instead". Existing saves keep what they have.
- [x] Stadium game days bring crowds, traffic and a lively look (G18)   → DONE [fix/vehicles-services]: game days (the existing daily draw) now run gates 16:30, kick-off 19:00, whistle 21:15; away fans drive in from out of town (10/28/64 cars by tier) and park on the plaza (drawn in rows), drive home after the whistle; the stands fill, the teams play under brighter floodlights, fans mill at the gates. Inspector shows the game day; remote `stadium_game {building}` puts one on.

# OPEN WORK — re-consolidated 2026-10-11 evening (the only open list)
Everything still open, from the old groups 6–8, the group 1–5 leftovers, todo6 (docs/playtest/todo_2026-10-11-evening.md) and flags H1–H4 (docs/playtest/flags_2026-10-11-evening.md), regrouped by the code each touches so one agent can own a group.

## A. Start screen, saves and performance
- [x] Clean start (Harvey 2026-10-15): when a game starts or loads, no map layer is on (today `Overlays::default` has `zoning: true`, crates/nd_app/src/render/overlays.rs ~68) and the tool is Inspect (screenshots show the Road tool's panel open after a load). Check what else carries over between loads (tool, selection, open windows, layer) and reset it to the plain map in Inspect. → DONE [round1/r3-visual]: no layer on by default (`Overlays::default` zoning off); `city_reset::catch_up` also turns every layer off (the other-projects switch kept), heat vision off and the tool to Inspect on every new game/load/generate (selection, route edit, follow already reset); the first city starts in Inspect too (ToolState default)
- [x] Full insight through the remote API (Harvey 2026-10-15): "you should be able to recover any data from the game if I ask". Today the sim keeps only a car's route ahead (a re-plan starts it afresh) and the "came from" line is the app's breadcrumb (`overlay/trails.rs`, last ~2 km, lost on load), so the API can't say where a car has been or why it turned. Add, in the sim and saved with the city (bounded): per trip a log of where it went (road pieces with times), every re-plan (when, where, why: stalled/full exit/lane change/crash/rage, what was avoided, old vs new remaining length), U-turns and dead-end turnarounds, stalls and their causes, anger changes, parking hunt steps; the same kind of history for people (trips, mode choices and why, failed routes) and key events for buildings. Expose it: `inspect {car|citizen|building, history:true}`, a trip replay as points, and a generic read-only query/dump (any entity by id, all fields) so nothing in the game is unreachable from the API. → DONE [round1/r5-insight]: `World::insight` (`crate::insight`, `world/insight.rs`), saved with the city as one compact string (varint byte streams per log, deflated, base64; old saves load with none, a damaged one loads empty), recording only (no RNG, nothing read back). Vehicles: each trip from taking to the road: street pieces entered with times (traffic notes at `Traffic::entered`), every re-plan with why (lane change, full exit, crash ahead, stuck, parking hunt, errand, new route, emergency), what it avoided and remaining metres before/after (one `note_replan` line each in `reroute_stalled`, `replan_from_here`, `follow`, `reroute`; behaviour unchanged), U-turns (dead end or not), rage U-turns, stalls >= 1 min with cause and the car in the way, anger in 10% steps, crashes, errand changes (the parking hunt's steps); finished trips kept with the driver or in a ring of 300. Citizens: each set-off (mode, expected minutes, runner-up, modes that could not be done), every activity change (legs, waits), failed routes with why, life events (arrived, home, job, car, household, crime victim, suspect, patient, crash), last drive. Buildings: started, built, demolition, business opened/closed/reopened, owner, rebuilt, households in/out, incidents and how they ended, renamed. Bounded per log (trip 3 KB, done trip 768 B, person 224+192 B, building 256 B; oldest street pieces drop first). API: `inspect {car|citizen|building, history: true}`, `replay {car|citizen}` (points with times), `get {kind, id, path}` / `get {field, path}` (any entity or any world field, all serialised fields), `query {kind, filter, fields}`; docs/REMOTE.md "History, replays and the full dump". The selected car's "came from" line uses the saved trip where the breadcrumbs don't reach (survives load). Measured on Harvey's day-62 save (16,660 people): recording ~1.1 ms of a ~60 ms tick (~2%); after two game days the memory holds ~5 MB and adds ~4.5-5 MB (~12%) to the 41 MB compressed save (`examples/insight_probe`)
- [x] Title screen as a multi-pane view: city thumbnails (most recent game first, largest); selecting a city lists its save states (latest, autosave, dated quick saves, history autosaves with day, population, time) to click before Load; Continue still loads the newest → DONE [w1/start-saves]: two panes (ui/title.rs): left one card per game grouped by game id (by city name for saves without one), the newest game large with its figures, the rest small; right the picked game's states newest first (latest quick save, autosave, dated quick saves, history autosaves, marked moments) with picture, day, population, money, kind and age; click then Load, or double-click; Continue unchanged (`saves::games`, `scan_history`)
- [x] New-map page as a step-by-step wizard that can't be skipped: landscape (preview) → starting city/preset → city name (required, suggested) → review → Start; Next disabled until the step is done → DONE [w1/start-saves]: ui/newgame.rs steps Landscape › Starting city › Name › Review with Back/Next and a step row; the starting city must be clicked (empty map counts), the name starts empty and is required (dice suggests); the mini-map shows on every step; reopening starts at step 1
- [x] A new game started from a preset/example gets its own name, never "Perfect AI City" (today's day-long game looked lost) → DONE [w1/start-saves]: cause: the bundled saves/Perfect_AI_City.ron carries the name and no game id, so loading and playing it made a game called Perfect AI City; now a save at day 1 with nobody living there and no id (or a starting town's name) loads as a new game with a new id and a suggested name (`saves::starter_of`); the wizard requires a name
- [x] Saves and flags folders resolve next to the exe, not the working directory → DONE [w1/start-saves]: `home::enter` makes the exe's folder the working directory first thing in main (`ND_HOME` overrides, `ND_HOME=.` keeps the cwd), so saves/, flags/, screenshots/, exports/, crash logs, assets/ (palette, rules), settings.ron and committee_settings.ron all live beside the exe; CLAUDE.md and REMOTE.md say so
- [x] Play freezes 2–3 s every 2 game hours: the history autosave of a ~240 MB city on the main thread; write it in the background → DONE [w1/start-saves]: measured, it was not the autosave (daily, already on a worker; its main-thread copy is ~90 ms) but the land value walk-time pass every two game hours: `amenity_minutes` searched the whole map for each grid cell over sea and open country, 1150 ms in one tick on the 17,627-person save; a bounded box query gives the same answer in 7.5 ms (perf_probe max tick 1267 → 410 ms; in game no frame over 500 ms through 11 game hours except midnight's 666 ms daily tick). The autosave's picture is filmed once (was twice) and Quick save writes on a worker too; status.render.graphics.long_frames lists hitches
- [x] Faster, smaller saves; lower load peak; fewer/compressed history autosaves (24 × ~150–240 MB per big city) → DONE [w1/start-saves]: saves are gzip (zlib-rs, fastest) of unindented RON: 239 MB → 37.5 MB; load 2.8 s against 7.2 s for the old plain file; the RON text held while loading is half (indentation was 124 of 239 MB) and the compressed bytes are dropped before parsing; history keeps the newest 8 plus 8 older ones 5 days apart (was 24); old plain saves load (`World::read_save`)
- [ ] Save → load → continue determinism (docs/review/save_determinism_wip.patch)
- [ ] Big-city performance 20k–50k people → PARTLY [w1/start-saves]: profiled the 17,631-person save (perf_probe now times every tick step): 84% of each tick was `step_tow_jobs`: every due tow call asked `tow_company_near`, which counted all tow jobs for every one of the 1,731 buildings, with 1,341 jobs queued (the city is far short of tow trucks); counting only at tow yards takes the mean tick from 158 to 27 ms (max 299 → 101 ms), same results. Left: step_citizens and step_traffic (~11 ms each), the 1,341-job tow queue itself (gameplay: too few tow trucks for the overflow parking), and 20k–50k cities untested
- [x] Settings menu (graphics, controls, UI scale) → DONE [w1/start-saves]: a Settings window in the top bar (`window {name: "settings"}`): graphics tier (now saved in settings.ron and used at start unless ND_QUALITY), text size, speech bubbles, and the camera and key reference; the View window points to it

## B. Rendering and visuals
- [x] Bug: cyclists' bike wheels are square (Harvey 2026-10-15). In the cyclist model (crates/nd_procgen/src/people.rs ~301–306) each wheel is a flat 0.68 m box (`b.part` from (x-0.34, 0) to (x+0.34, 0.68)), and the frame is two bars. Make round wheels (thin tyre rings with a few spokes or a disc, 16+ sides close up) and a real frame (diamond tube shape, seat, bars). The vehicle bikes/scooters in vehicles.rs use 8-sided wheels: raise to 12–16. → DONE [round1/r3-visual]: 16-sided tyre rings (both faces and tread) with three spokes, a diamond frame of round tubes in the rider's accent colour, fork, stem, bars and saddle (people.rs, `MAX_RIDER_TRIS` 190→440); vehicle bikes/scooters 8→14-sided wheels
- [ ] Graphics fidelity track (Harvey 2026-10-15: no hand-made assets; keep adding detail procedurally; our own Nanite-like system). Stay on Bevy.
  - **Art direction: stylised, gritty, not cartoonish** (Harvey 2026-10-15: "still stylized but not cartoonish"). Keep a clear stylised look (strong shapes, a deliberate palette, neon) but grounded: believable proportions and materials, weathering and grime, muted surfaces under the neon, real-world light; no toy-like bright flat colours or chunky cartoon shapes. That is the long-term goal every step aims at. Don't go all-out on detailing yet: move towards it step by step, keeping the game fast.
  - **Rule (Harvey 2026-10-15): every detail is permanent and the same from every camera.** No props that appear only in Walk mode or only near the camera (the walk-only street clutter: signs, carts, vending, steam, hanging cables, puddles, scooters, cracks, was removed for this). Ground level gets better by improving the buildings, streets and graphics themselves; distance detail (LOD / our own virtual geometry) may simplify far things, never invent near ones.
  - **Detail in the generators:** window frames and recesses, ledges, cornices, balconies, AC units, pipes, rooftop clutter, bevelled edges, awnings, shopfront detail; style by style, within triangle budgets that the LOD below makes affordable.
  - **Procedural materials:** generated (not hand-made) textures and normal maps for brick, concrete, glass, metal, tiles; grime, rain streaks and wear by building age and condition; procedural decals (graffiti, posters, stains).
  - **Lighting:** light spilling from windows and signs onto streets at night, reflections on wet streets and glass, volumetric light shafts and fog, a better sky and sun; use what Bevy already offers before writing our own.
  - **Detail by distance and our own "Nanite":** level-of-detail versions of every generated mesh, impostors for distant buildings, and a cluster-based virtual-geometry renderer (meshes split into small clusters with simplified parents, picked per frame by screen size, GPU-culled) so close-ups can be far richer at city scale. Check Bevy's own experimental meshlet / virtual geometry support first and build on it if it fits; otherwise write our own.
- [ ] Future-city looks (Harvey 2026-10-15):
  - **Holographic billboards** where they fit (neon strip, tourist quarter, tower tops, busy junctions): big animated holograms floating over streets and between towers (fish, dancers, ads for the city's own companies), brighter at night.
  - **Skyways between towers:** some tall building designs include glass sky bridges at height; and when one owner (developer, company) has tall buildings close together it can add a bridge between them. People walk across them (part of the walk graph at their height).
  - **Vending machines and capsule hotels:** vending walls and robot kiosks on street corners and in stations (a little business of their own); capsule hotels as a hotel kind in dense districts (stacked pods, cheap beds for visitors and workers).
  - **Vertical farms that look cool:** the existing vertical farm (BusinessKind::VertFarm) gets a striking design: glass towers of stacked grow floors glowing magenta and green at night, misting, drones tending racks.
  - **Smog days:** a visible smog layer whose thickness follows pollution, weather and wind: good clear days and bad brown days like LA, with a smog forecast, health and mood effects and fewer people out on bad days.
- [ ] Underground first (Harvey 2026-10-15: before an undercity): build out the subway visually (stations with platforms, halls, stairs and escalators, tunnels and trains seen in the Underground view) and improve how underground paths and roads render (tunnel walls, lights, portals, people and cars inside), so the underworld can grow from there later.
- [x] Bug: a new coal plant smokes before it's finished (Harvey 2026-10-15: "the smoke came out of nowhere, before the building was finished"). The smoke (crates/nd_app/src/render/haze.rs ~170–182) skips only plants with `b.construction` set, and placed power plants seem never to get a construction stage in the sim (only a test sets one), so the plumes start the moment the plant is placed, whatever is drawn. It also burns at 55% at least even with no staff (`intensity = 0.55 + …`, staffing clamped to 0.3). Fix: smoke only once the plant is built and actually generating (staffed, on the grid); check whether placed plants should get a proper build time (scaffold, then fire up), and start the plume gently when it first fires up. → DONE [round1/r3-visual]: smoke only from an operating plant (not while its lot is cleared or built) that makes power and has staff, 0.3..1 with staffing × grid draw; the plume eases in over ~half a minute of scene time when it fires up (`haze::plant_smoke`, `ease_smoke`). Placed plants still get no build stage in the sim (left as is: power tests expect them working at once)
- [x] Weather pauses with the game, and a "Show weather" toggle (Harvey 2026-10-15). Snow and rain keep falling while paused because the weather effects animate on real time (`time.elapsed_secs()` in crates/nd_app/src/render/climate.rs, ~158 and ~288): drive them from the game clock (and game speed) so they freeze when paused and speed up with fast-forward. Add a "Show weather" checkbox to the View window's ALSO DRAWN section (ui/windows.rs `land`) that hides falling snow/rain, fog and puddles visually without changing the simulated weather. → DONE [round1/r3-visual]: `climate::SceneClock`: game-driven animation clock (0 paused, √(speed/16×) held to 0.25..6×) for rain/snow, hot-spot steam, coal smoke and airlift rotors; "Show weather" checkbox in View › ALSO DRAWN (`Lighting::show_weather`, remote `lighting {weather}`) hides rain/snow, the weather fog and wet streets only (`precip_shown`)
- [x] Chinook (airlift) rotors look strange (Harvey 2026-10-15). They're drawn in crates/nd_app/src/render/airlift.rs: each rotor is three flat 9 m boxes plus a hub (`rotor_mesh`), spun at a fixed 14 rad/s of real time (~2.2 turns a second; at 60 fps a blade jumps ~13° a frame, so it strobes, and TAA smears it), in the same Panel material as the fuselage. Fix: a blurred rotor disc (a faint translucent disc with soft blade streaks) once spinning fast, thin tapered blades at low speed, spin tied to the game speed, front and rear rotors meshing (counter-rotating, offset in phase and height like a real tandem helicopter, without the blades crossing each other), and a dark blade material. Ask Harvey for a screenshot of what looks off if unsure. → DONE [round1/r3-visual]: thin tapered drooping blades in a dark blade material; counter-rotating, mirror-phased tandem rotors that never cross (tested over a full turn), rear 1.1 m higher; a faint translucent disc with three soft streaks once a frame turns them past 0.11 rad; spin on the scene clock (still when paused)
- [ ] Drive mode (Harvey 2026-10-15): a third camera mode next to Orbit and Walk in the top bar, where the player drives a car around the city at car speed. Reuse the chase camera (crates/nd_app/src/chase.rs) for the view and Walk mode's input handling (`CameraMode` in camera.rs). The player's car is a real car in the traffic model so others see it, queue behind it and give way, and it follows lanes, junctions, ramps and tunnels with simple arcade handling (W/S throttle and brake, A/D steer, kept to the road surface; collisions bump rather than wreck); optional assist that keeps to the lane. Speedometer and the street name shown; Esc or Tab back to Orbit at the same spot.
- [x] Street lights only lit near the middle of the view when zoomed out: pick lit lamps by the camera frustum/zoom; past a distance draw them cheaply (glow sprites) instead of dropping them → DONE [w1/render]: real lights go to the lamps in the camera frustum nearest the eye (was: round the orbit target); every lamp also throws an additive ground pool (merged meshes per 300 m patch, on the surface under it) that fades in where the real lights stop and out into the fog; lamp heads now follow the street profile (they stayed at flat height on hills). Shots O:/nd_w1render/run/screenshots/{before,after2}_h22_d*.png

- [x] Landmark neon glows by day → DONE [w1/render]: attraction glow is marked (NEON_MARK) and the window shader dims it by day to about the signs' level; the observatory beam (NIGHT_MARK) is off by day. Shots {before,after2}_pier_h13.png

Test steep_junctions_leave_no_gaps (top-down coverage). Not checked visually on a real steep junction

Shots {before,after2}_metro_under_d250.png

- [x] Capsule district (Harvey 2026-10-12: "a dragonball style district ... super high tech stuff only. White domes of different sizes, some on stilts like popsicles, usually round windows on the perimeter") → DONE [feat/capsule-look]: new district look `Look::Capsule` (appended; old saves load unchanged), picked as "Capsule" for homes and/or shops in the District tool's policy editor (remote `district set {residential: capsule, commercial: capsule}`); shops take offices and gadget shops only where the market carries them (`Look::favours_only`), never built Low tier, lifts land value a little. Ten styles (`buildings/capsule.rs`, appended to `Style`): dome house, saucer house, stilt dome (ball on a single stilt), twin domes, dome with pods, capsule (pill) tower, saucer stack, stilt tower, dome hall (glass drum, striped awnings, glass vault entrance), dome campus (glass ribbons, radar ball); smooth surfaces of revolution with coloured crown caps, porthole or round-ended slot bands lit by the window system, pastel stripes with a night glow line, round doors in entry pods, glass link tubes, radar balls, dishes, round landing pads with hover cars; lawns, hedges, round planters, ball trees, palms, globe lamps (`kit/domes.rs`). Towers with a sim rooftop pad keep the look with their own deck. Spotless (never weathered). Tests `capsule_tests` (odd lots × heights × tiers, sites, coplanar, heights follow floors, triangle budget, menus, pads). Shots O:/nd_targets/capsule_run/screenshots/v5{day,night}_*.png. - [x] Follow mode as a GTA-style chase camera (Harvey 2026-10-14: "keep the camera lower behind the car like GTA, pointed in the same direction as it drives and clip any buildings that collide between camera and car. same game-style view for people following") → DONE [feat/chase-cam]: Follow on anything with a heading is a chase camera (`chase.rs`): low behind it (car 3.1 m up / 7.5 m back, walker 2.0 / 3.5, cyclist 2.2 / 4.0, robot, bus/lorry/fire engine, camper, train, metro, ship by length, aircraft further), looking where it faces and a little ahead; heading eased and rate-limited (no jitter at a standstill, a walker standing keeps the last heading, a reversing car keeps the camera behind its nose, a flipped heading swings round over ~1.3 s), floor eased over decks, swings in from where the camera was. Right-drag or Q/E turns it round the subject (eases back after 1.2 s), wheel or [ ] zooms 0.55–3×, F (or the banner's High view / Chase view) toggles the old high orbit follow; Tab POV and Esc as before. Indoors people get the high view. Occlusion: the eye keeps above the terrain and pulls in where a hill is in the way; scenery meshes (buildings and cranes, streets with decks/lamps/poles, trees, park and vacant dressing) whose bounds reach the line of sight wear a cutting copy of their material while chasing (`render/cutaway.rs`: StandardMaterial + an extension discarding a dithered cone eye→subject above a floor line, main pass and prepass; window glow cuts in its own shader); only the main camera's views cut (shadows kept). Into a road tunnel or the metro the Underground view switches on, and back off. Remote `follow {view, turn, tilt, zoom, cutaway}`, `status.follow_view/chasing/render.cut_meshes`. Cost: ~40 meshes swapped, frame time within noise (26.5 vs 26.6 ms, High). Tests chase::tests (pose, swing, jitter, flip, intro, ground, cut, occluder pick), cutaway tests (swap on/off/heat, WGSL validates with naga). Shots O:/nd_targets/chase_shots. Left: on footbridge stairs the deck can hide a walker's body (cone stops short of the subject)

## C. Roads, junctions and traffic
- [ ] Rework interchanges as an "interchange stamp" tool (Harvey 2026-10-15: with the two default highways the current Interchange tool "doesn't really work at all"). Today's tool (`add_interchange`, layouts street_under = diamond with the highway lifted, and street_over) plans ramps for a street crossing one highway; parclo/cloverleaf were never done. Wanted: pick the highway(s) and stamp a complete interchange, previewed with its cost, footprint and refusals before Implement, sized to the highway lanes, grade-separated with bridges, ramps, merges and signals as needed, and buildings it would clear shown in red.
  - **Two highways meeting** (pick both, or the point where they cross or would meet): cloverleaf (four loop ramps), stack (two- to four-level flyovers), turbine/whirlpool (sweeping curved ramps), trumpet (one highway ending at another, T shape), directional T.
  - **A highway and a new perpendicular road** (pick one highway and a spot; the tool adds the cross road, at a chosen length and lanes, and its ramps): diamond, partial cloverleaf (parclo), full cloverleaf, single-point urban interchange (SPUI), diverging diamond, roundabout interchange (dumbbell/dogbone).
  - Works on the generated default highways and any the player draws; refuses with the reason (too close to another interchange, slope, water, not enough room).
  - See Extras: CSUR-style road lanes (tapers, ramps as splits/merges, gore markings) would make the stamped ramps clean.
- [x] U-turns at signalled junctions (Harvey 2026-10-15): allow a U-turn from the left-turn lane at any signalled junction where the street has 2 or more lanes in each direction (today `traffic/build.rs` allows U-turns only at dead ends: "U-turns only at dead ends"). It runs on the left-turn phase, so the signal timing and conflict checks need to cover it; the route planner can then use it. → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: a U-turn connector from the leftmost lane of every street with 2+ lanes each way at a real junction (3+ arms, not a ring or highway), into the inner lane going back (`Lane::uturn`); driven as a left turn (its phase and arrow, giving way to oncoming traffic, counted in the signal demand); open only while the junction is signalled and the lane keeps its left arrow (`LaneNetwork::refresh_uturns`, re-run on rebuild, junction rules and new arrows; cars routed through a closed one plan again). It asks for no arrow of its own (`exits_of` leaves it out). The planner uses it at `UTURN_COST` 60 m plus 30 m a vehicle on the lane it turns back into. Into the kerb lane (first try) the turners stood at the line waiting for room behind parked and delivering vans and locked Harvey's avenue at (320,-400); a turner held at the line (exit full, no gap) now gives the U-turn up and goes another way. Tests `traffic/tests/uturns.rs`
- [x] Cars loop through dead ends after re-planning (flag day 62 02:36, Kade "Zero" Castellano's hatchback 134:757 at (313,-395), going home Onyx Garden → Saffron Block, 1,708 min on the road against 201 expected, anger 80%): his trail shows 5–6 out-and-back spurs off one jammed avenue (a round cul-de-sac, dead-end side streets). Cause, from the code: a car stuck at the head of a lane re-plans from where it stands (`Traffic::reroute_stalled` in traffic/routing.rs, `route_avoiding`), the new route must start facing forward, and the only turn-round places are dead ends, so it is sent to the nearest stub, comes back into the same jam and repeats; re-plans don't remember what was tried and weigh distance over queues; angry drivers add illegal mid-street U-turns (`rage_u_turns`, world/tow_moves.rs). Same as Theo Achebe's parking loop (fix/bridge-riders left it open). Fix ideas: the U-turn item above; re-plans remember recent detours and don't turn back without need; queues weigh more; re-plan less eagerly in a jam. → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: re-plans (`reroute_stalled`, the missed-turn re-plan at the solid line, the half-hour `replan_from_here`) use `LaneNetwork::route_replan`: queues weigh 20 m a vehicle (6 before) and turning back (a dead end's turning circle, a U-turn) 250 m, and they never go back into what the car gave up on in the last 900 traffic s (`Car::avoided`, at most 6; forgotten when lanes rebuild). Plus the U-turns above. Waiting out a missed turn in a jam (a detour cap) was tried and dropped: on his city it made drivers angrier and trips longer. Measured with `examples/deadend_probe` on a copy of his day-62 autosave, two game days from midnight, main before → this branch merged with main: dead-end turnarounds 3,478 → 2,801, trips with 2+ dead-end turnarounds 257 → 128, trips done 7,690 → 8,056, mean trip 189 → 168 min (p90 2.89 → 2.43 of expected), junction U-turns 0 → 1,658; trips still on the road at 3x their expected time 767 → 822 and rage U-turns 1,549 → 2,450 went up: left. Kade's own loop (out and back to the dead end at (619,-455), node 1606, the city's worst) is the same mechanism.
- [x] TOP (flag 2026-10-12, day 29 at (202,106)): 5 emergency vehicles stuck 12–40 game h "braking for a jaywalker" → DONE [fix/walk-networks]: not the tunnel (nobody could use the tunnel then: tunnels were left out of the walk graph and of foot routes) but bikes at the roundabout above it: a rider gave way to anyone on a crossing it rode over, bikes included; bikes riding round the ring stood on an arm's crosswalk (so holding it) giving way to bikes standing on the ring's crossing, which gave way to them. On his 09:20 autosave it re-formed within 12 game hours (600 bikes at three spots, 18 vehicles incl. 5 sirens standing). Riders now give way to walkers only (`crossing_taken`); two game days on the save: no vehicle stands > 300 s there. The watchdog treated giving way as a jam's victim and never looked; service vehicles queued for 3 h are now sent on, 6 h taken off the road (`QUEUED_REROUTE/CLEAR`). With tunnels now walkable: people in a tunnel are drawn at its depth (riders found by the road they ride along, walkers by underground walk nodes), shown in the underground view, and ignore the crossings and level crossings above (`World::leg_in_tunnel`)
- [x] Pedestrian connections not connecting; a layer showing separate walk networks; underground paths little used (Harvey 2026-10-12) → DONE [fix/walk-networks]: cause: the walk graph (and the foot router) left out every tunnel, so his east district (64 buildings, ~460 residents), joined to town only by a 1 km Mixed 2+2 street tunnel, and Hotwell 3/5/6 + Cable Yard, joined only by foot tunnels, were islands on foot, and his pedestrian tunnels were never walked. Pedestrian tunnels and street tunnels set to Mixed are walked now (`Segment::walkable`; car-only and pre-Use tunnels stay cars only), their walk nodes at the bore's depth. His save: walk networks with buildings 9 → 6 (main + 4 public beaches behind private lots, by design + Iron Containers on an unjoined street, road-cut too); a game day after: ~2,070 people walk/ride through tunnels (the street tunnel 129k person-ticks, the foot tunnels 2–8k each), before 0. New map layer "Walk networks" (Traffic): every walkway in its network's colour (ids kept across rebuilds, a merge keeps the larger one's), buildings off the main network outlined, legend by size (hover names, click flies there); remote `walk_networks`; "Walk networks" buttons on cut-off inspector lines and no-route/stranded alerts. Probe `examples/walknet_probe`
- [x] TOP: Road type is inferred from lanes (`is_highway_spec`): a 3-lane one-way street silently becomes a highway, losing sidewalk and zones, and can't get them back. Presets (Path / Street / On-ramp / Highway) only set values and highlight when matched; explicit "Use" dial: Pedestrians only / Mixed / Car-only (sets speed, sidewalk, zoning); lanes never change the use; stored on the road (old saves keep their highways); Edit can switch an existing road's use (Harvey 2026-10-11) → DONE [w1/roads]: `roads::RoadUse` (pedestrian/mixed/car_only) stored on the road (`Gesture::Road::car_only`, `Segment::car_only`, serde default None = read from the lanes as before, so old saves keep their highways); a 3-lane one-way Mixed street keeps pavements, street speed, junctions and lots. Road tool and Edit panel: presets Path/Street/On-ramp/Highway only set the dials and light only on an exact match ("custom" otherwise), a Use dial, lanes forward/back 0–4 that never change the Use; Edit can switch an existing road's Use (pavements back). Remote: `tool {use}`, `add_road {use}`, new `road_use {gesture, use, lanes_fwd, lanes_bwd}`; gestures report `use`/`use_set`. Tests: road_use_tests (3-lane mixed keeps pavements/lots, car-only fronts nothing, old save stays a highway and loads so), app preset highlight test
- [x] Harvey's city gridlocked (away fans and crane boxes crawl): find the choke points → DONE [w1/roads]: headless `examples/gridlock_probe` on a copy of his save. Causes: (1) walkers against the light stepped out in front of cars standing at the line on their green (only moving cars counted as traffic): cars stood 300–560 s at signalled junctions, queues back across the grid; now they leave a car 5 s at the line the road; (2) a steady stream at an unsignalled zebra kept drivers standing for minutes: after 20 s waiting the walkers coming up hold back (not counted as waiting, so drivers do not stop for them); (3) away fans were all set down on one kerb spot by the stadium and stood an hour waiting to pull out: they leave from up to four free kerb spots, stragglers keep leaving after 22:00. Mean vehicles standing >120 s, his city, merged main → w1/roads: game evening 16:00–23:00 19.1 → 10.1 (pre-merge base 30.0 → 2.7), morning 07:00–11:00 6.0 → 0.0, afternoon 15:00–20:00 3.6 → 0.6. Port lorries: no choke found (freight standing 0–5 of ~55). Left: the tow-job queue A found is not touched
- [x] Curved through-roads not mended on Implement → DONE [w1/roads]: before the build, a curved road through one of the project's junctions gets a control point there, the drawn curve pinned around it so it strays < 0.2 m, and the other roads there are put on the same point; a road snapped onto a curve's vertex counts as running through (test `a_curved_road_through_junctions_gets_its_points`)
- [ ] Backland often refused rather than joined; F27 diagonal sliver parcel can't merge (concave) → LEFT [round1/r1-roads] (looked at, not done): joins are refused where the joined outline has a sharp inner corner (`zoning/join.rs`, `reflex_vertex(.., 0.6)`) because a lot is framed on one rectangle (`Lot::corners`) and buildings are sized to it: on a concave lot they would overhang the notch. Doing it needs footprints fitted inside a concave lot (the largest rectangle in it, or an L/U footprint) before the joins can allow concave unions. Related, done: a U-shaped rest round a kept lot is split at its inner corners (K9)
- [x] Flag day 46 14:31 (322,-172) "i don't see anyone in the road, everyone is taking the footpath": cars on Zenith Circus braking "for someone crossing the road" under a footbridge → DONE [fix/bridge-riders]: bikes wheeled over a footbridge's deck (a step beside the crosswalk it replaced) were matched to that crosswalk by `rider_stop`'s geometric search and claimed it, so the cars under the bridge emergency-braked. Riders on a raised walkway leg (`World::leg_raised`) now skip crossings and level crossings like tunnel riders, and riders on an elevated path / viaduct / bridge only meet crossings of streets at their own level (`leg_way_height`, `crossing_apart_from`, `LEVEL_APART` 3 m). On a copy of his save (600 ticks from 14:20): car-ticks braking at bridged crossings 5,510 → 623 city-wide, 430 → 78 within 40 m of the flag (crossing 382: all gone). The jam explanation now names the person (`StopReason::Pedestrians/Jaywalker { crossing }`, `World::stopped_for`, `stop_reason_text`; API `why.by` / `by_kind: citizen`; inspector link). Tests `world/bridge_riders_tests.rs`.
- [x] → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: rings of decks are ridden round by the walk graph's way over (`bike_over_footbridges`, a bounded detour), and a ride planned before a bridge went up is fitted to the bridges again once after the walk graph changes (`Rider::bridges`). Was: Bikes still cross at grade where a footbridge stands: ends joined into a ring of decks are not spliced into bike paths (`bike_over_footbridges`), and riders whose path was planned before a bridge (or crossing a crosswalk a nearby bridge closed) don't replan as walkers do. The residual ~620 car-ticks per 600 ticks in Harvey's city (crossings 649, 383, 366, 509) are these.
- [x] Flag day 44 03:53 (38.7,394.9) Theo Achebe's sedan "racing", "raging because it took too long", status "out to the entertainment, looking for parking" → DONE [fix/bridge-riders]: he was not racing (no gang role; a reckless 19-year-old in a tuned sedan at night): he was on a 10-hour drive to Cabaret Afterglow, 4+ hours of it hunting for parking, so the rage was real. Causes fixed: a worn-out hunter (past `HUNT_GIVE_UP`) who gave up in a queue was sent off again to a lot across town, and a hunter crawling round a loop of jams never stood still long enough to give up; now worn-out hunters take no lots, and past `HUNT_ABANDON` (2 h) they leave the car as soon as they are slow (replayed from his day-44 save: parked at 01:15 instead of 06:06). Racers: never timed while racing (already), and a racer's drive home is now timed afresh (no stale race-start clock); a loser drives home sore (`SORE_LOSER` anger, `DriveState::sore_loser`, "lost the street race" in the inspector, API `trip.anger_cause` `late`/`lost_race`). Journey status for racers, takeover crews and moto packs says what they are doing ("street racing", "on the way to a street race", "at the party after the street race", "in a street takeover"...), in the inspector's driver line and the API's `journey.purpose`.
- [x] Edit road-upgrade panel still uses the old presets → DONE [w1/roads]: presets / Use / lanes forward-back / level (lower, raise, ground, tunnel) / straight-curved, as the Road tool; roundabouts get 1–3 lanes and a level
- [x] Lit off-ramp crossing has no painted zebra → DONE [w1/roads]: an off-ramp's mouth at a signalled junction (the walk graph's lit crossing) gets the zebra and its walk-light posts (`street::junction::lit_ramp_mouth`)
- [~] Interchanges: parclo/cloverleaf and street-over-highway layouts; Interchange tool ignores pending roads in some cases → PARTLY [w1/roads]: street-over-highway layout (`InterchangeLayout::StreetOver`: the street on a bridge 7 m over the highway, ramps climbing to it; Interchange panel "Street under / Street over", remote `add_interchange {layout}`); pending roads now checked as built (a curve sampled, a planned roundabout's ring) — a planned curve sweeping across a ramp was missed. Left: parclo/cloverleaf loop ramps
- [x] Raised / tunnel roundabouts with [ and ] → DONE [w1/roads]: `Gesture::Roundabout::level` / `Ring::level` (serde default 0); [ and ] raise and lower the ring in the Road tool (Edit panel too, remote `add_roundabout {level}`); a raised or sunken ring takes the roads that reach it at about its level and lets the rest pass under/over; one spanning the lots or in a tunnel takes no lots (test `a_raised_roundabout_takes_the_roads_at_its_level_and_passes_over_the_rest`)
- [x] Nothing may be built over a road: marinas (H3), dams (H1) and other placeables refused on roads, and no junction made → DONE [w1/roads]: `World::road_under`: every Build-window placeable (lots/sites incl. plants, services, attractions, depots, parking, cargo hub; docks incl. marinas; stations and yards — their own track excepted) is refused in the preview and on the click when its footprint takes in a carriageway, built or planned in the project (tunnels and lot-spanning decks pass; footpaths left out), with the spot. A dock's access stub ends on the first street it meets (a T, never a crossroads). Dams: D's dam tool owns its road check (no duplicate). citygen keeps rail buildings and docks off its streets (a railside street stretch gives way to the central station/yard; the ferry walks the shore further); a rail facility that fails after insertion is taken back

## D. Waterfront, parks and attractions
- [x] Dam: two-step tool (place the building, then the wall coast to coast, one end near the building) with live size, cost and expected power; dams carry footpaths only, no roads (H1) → DONE [w1/waterfront]: placing a hydro plant opens the wall step (Buildings panel + map preview: the arch from the first bank to the pointer, green/red, with "wall N m (M m over the water) · costs X · about Y MW expected"); right click re-picks the first bank, Esc leaves it for later (the plant makes nothing until its wall stands; inspector "Draw its dam wall"). Rules: both ends on dry bank within 30 m of the water, one within 50 m of the plant, across the plant's own river once and roughly square, at most 260 m, clear of every road/rail/path, 40 m from another dam; 240 a metre. The wall carries a footpath along its crest (in the wall's price); a street/highway/rail over a dam is refused on implement ("Dams carry footpaths only"), tunnels pass. Old plants keep the automatic arch ("Redraw its dam wall" replaces it, e.g. Millrace 2 in H1). Remote `dams`, `dam {building, a, b, build}` and the tool step `dam {step, cursor, click}`; `place {await_wall}`.
- [x] Public beaches side by side merge into one beach whose landscape fits the combined shape; umbrellas packed away at night (H2) → DONE [w1/waterfront]: beach lots sharing 2 m+ of edge are one beach: one state (held by the oldest), one name on every lot, crowd/capacity/access/contamination/tourist pull over the whole sand, each lot drawing its share of the crowd; planned as one (one tier, towel spots up to the seams, sand without the inset strip, lifeguard tower and closed sign only on the anchor lot). After dark umbrellas and towels are packed away (loungers stacked).

A route choice for citizens like the metro.

## G. Economy and businesses (big features)
- [ ] Mega-blocks (Harvey 2026-10-15): huge self-contained buildings with homes, shops, offices and parks inside, that only a mega-corporation can build (on land it has assembled), as its flagship project; they house thousands and change the district around them.
- [x] TOP: Close the money loop for rent and property (Harvey 2026-10-15: "people already pay rents, to who? this whole economy needs some holes fixed"). What the code does today: each day a household pays `rent_due` out of its money (world/trips/household.rs ~96–105) and that money goes nowhere; only properties owned by a resident (OwnerKind::Household / Person) pass a share of the rent to the owner's household (`INVEST_RENT_NET`, world/rich.rs ~150–190). Rent on buildings owned by the out-of-town developers, the university, mega-corps and "independent landlords" (no owner) simply vanishes, and those owners have no money of their own (`estate::Owner` has only spent / income_day / earned). Wanted, like real life:
  - Every property has an owner with an account: a resident, a company or the city. Rent goes to the owner; the owner pays upkeep, property tax, loans and wages, and keeps or reinvests the rest.
  - Residents can buy properties and rent them out. A resident who builds an apartment building starts a company to do it; the tenants pay rent to that company, and if it works the company grows: buys more plots, builds more, or builds a commercial building or strip mall to rent to other businesses.
  - Businesses that rent their unit pay rent to the building's owner (ties to several businesses per building).
  - No money created or destroyed except at the edges (wages from outside, exports/imports, taxes to the city): a City Data money-flow page shows where rent, wages and profits go.
  - **Ledger (Harvey 2026-10-15: "money can't disappear… all transactions logged in a ledger, efficiently"):** a double-entry money system for the whole sim.
    - **Accounts:** every household, business, company/owner, the city treasury, and one "outside world" account (wages from out of town, imports/exports, tourists, out-of-town haulers). Every balance lives in one place.
    - **One door for money:** all money moves only through a single `transfer(from, to, amount, kind)` call (rent, wages, purchases, fares, taxes, fees, upkeep, construction, loans, fines, theft...). Replace every direct `money +=` / `money -=` in the sim (there are dozens: bots, crime, trash, car market, parking, construction, enforcement, air, campus...) with it.
    - **Efficient logging:** no row per transaction. Keep per-day totals by (kind, from-class, to-class) in a small fixed array (cheap to add to, cheap to save), daily history of those totals, and a short ring buffer of recent detailed transactions per account (last ~50) for the inspector and API. Detail on demand: a debug "full trace" switch for one account.
    - **Conservation check:** the sum of all balances plus the outside world is constant except for the known edges; a test runs the demo city for days and asserts it to the cent, and a debug check flags any tick where it drifts (and which kind caused it).
    - **Where it shows:** a City Data money-flow page (where rent, wages, profits and taxes go, a Sankey-style picture); each household, business and company shows its account and recent transactions; remote `ledger {account | kind | day}`. Lets the player see citizens build and grow companies or go out of business, and how policies help or hurt.
  - → DONE [round2/e1-ledger]: a double-entry ledger (`crates/nd_sim/src/bank.rs`, `world/ledger.rs`). Accounts: every household, citizen pocket, business, owner (firms, developers, residents' property companies get `Owner::money`; mega-corps use `MegaCorp::cash`, the university `Campus::funds`), tower developer, tourist, the air taxi company, one pool for the independent landlords, the treasury and the Outside World. Balances are exact `i64` ten-thousandths of a dollar; the old money fields are mirrors written after every move, so reads did not change. One door, `World::transfer(from, to, amount, kind)` (plus `pay_city` / `city_pays` for the treasury's itemised flows; `Economy::credit` is the treasury↔outside door): 135 direct money writes in 43 files now go through it, 18 treasury credits/debits that had a payer in town now name it (`pay_city`), the other 44 are the treasury↔outside door, and a business swapped in a building goes through `set_business`. 31 private kinds besides the treasury's 24 flows. Logging: today's totals by (kind, payer class, payee class) in one dense array, closed at the treasury's midnight into a sparse day summary (60 days saved, ~20 KB raw / 3.7 KB gz in Harvey's 46.5 MB save), a ring of the last 50 moves per account (24 for pockets and tourists; not saved), a full trace of one account on demand. A mirror changed outside the door is booked as `untracked` and reported (account, amount, last kind); `ledger {check: true}` / `ND_LEDGER_CHECK=1` reconciles every account every tick. Conservation: the sum of all balances plus the outside world is zero at every tick (test `sim_checks::the_ledger_balances_to_the_unit...`, 8 demo days, every tick reconciled, zero untracked; Harvey's day-61 save 2 days with the per-tick check: zero untracked, zero orphans). Old saves open their books from their money fields (`ledger_tests::an_old_save_opens...`). Rent: every rent goes to the building's owner (`collect_rent`): resident, company, developer, corp, university, or the landlords' pool; the owner pays 30% upkeep (out of town) and 10% property tax (new treasury flow `PropertyTax`), residents and their companies 15% mortgage, and keeps the rest (`INVEST_RENT_NET` is now just that sum, 45%). Developers and the landlords' pool send what they hold over $20,000 out of town daily (`profits_paid_out`). A resident's second home is bought by their property company (`OwnerKind::Company`, "Okafor Properties"): deposit from the household, the bank lends the rest, tenants pay the company, which pays the founder half its day's net and keeps half for the next deposit. Businesses renting their unit (no owner, or a developer's/university's/company's building) pay `shop_rent` to the owner: a tenth of a home's rent per job, but at most half the day's profit (a losing day pays none), kept per business in `Business::rent_day` so a building of several tenants sums them. Where it shows: remote `ledger {account | kind | day | flows | trace | check}` (docs/REMOTE.md); the person pane (household purse and pocket), business inspector (account, rent) and ownership section (owner's account, rent today/yesterday) show the balance and recent moves; City Data › Economy › Money flow draws each group's flows payer→payee as bands with the table under it; the Economy ledger drill-down is fed by the same door. Measured on Harvey's day-61 save over 3 days, before → after: treasury +566k → +637k (property tax ~$42k/day), broke businesses 16/14/14 → 16/14/13, closed 78/79/80 → same, homeless 2275/2164/2429 → 2275/2164/2428, population 16320 → 16304, rent-stressed households 565/632/686 → 565/632/721 (the runs part on day 3: residents' rent now reaches their purses and companies, so investors' money differs), business cash −76k over 3 days (shop rent). demo_sweep 1 16: day 8 19.2% → 19.2%, days 18-20 7.6% → 8.9% (seed 5 12.1% → 29.3%, the rest within a point or two either way); with shop rent switched off every seed matches main exactly, so the rest of the ledger changes nothing in the demo. A full shop rent (not capped by profit) gave 10.4%. Tick cost: ~119k moves a day at ~116 ns = 0.014 s of ~80 s a game day (0.02%); the daily reconcile 1.5 ms.
  - Left: ~~wages still come in from outside and payrolls go out (both booked), not employer to worker~~ (→ [p1-sim-leftovers], below); walk-in sales, repairs and hauliers' fees come from outside; residents do not yet build apartment blocks (they buy them through their company), and company growth beyond the next deposit is Round 3 (G1); "independent landlords" are one pool, not people; recent-move rings start empty after a load.
- [x] Malls, mega-malls and strip malls (Harvey 2026-10-15: "I don't see a lot of malls"). Today a mall is only a building look (the `CantileverMall` style in nd_procgen buildings/style.rs); the sim has no mall business: every shop building holds one business. Add:
  - **Strip malls (LA style):** 2–3 storey buildings with several shops side by side (a row of storefronts, signs above each door) and a small car park in front for about 10–20 cars, on commercial road plots; common, cheap, spread along main streets.
  - **Malls:** one building holding many shops (ties to "several businesses in one building" and mall bases in supertalls) with their own car park.
  - **Mega-malls:** a successful mall buys up adjacent land (the land-assembly item) and grows into a mega-mall with a big car park or parking structure, anchor stores, food court, and draws shoppers and traffic from across town.
  - → DONE [g2-land-malls]: three new `estate::Form`s (`StripMall`, `Mall`, `MegaMall`, appended; `world/malls.rs`), each cut into its own units (`malls::mall_layout`; `Unit::role` Shop / Anchor / FoodCourt, serde default) and let by the tenants machinery (`let_units`: anchors to a grocery or gadget store, the food court to cafés and bakeries, a mall's shops from the whole shop menu; malls let up to two units a day each, on an allowance of their own, `MALL_LETS_PER_DAY` = 2). **Strip malls:** a new commercial building on a road plot at least 20 m wide and 22 m deep (not high tier) is one by a hash of its seed, 45% on a main street (2+ lanes), 15% elsewhere, times the district story's `Story::mall_bias` (Quiet Acres 1.6, Afterglow Mile / Neo Tokyo 1.3 … Lantern Lanes 0.3, Old Town Charter 0); 2–3 storeys, a row of shopfronts (one per 7 m, 3–8) under offices, 10–20 parking spaces; its own shop or office keeps one unit. In a town already built, one old low shop or office building a day (chance 0.5; standing 20+ days, nobody renting in it, buyable) on such a plot is rebuilt as a strip mall, a shop on a plot of 1,100 m²+ as a mall (`rebuild_strip_malls`). Only from 3,000 people (`MALL_MIN_POP`): in the demo a row of strip malls let at once broke too many shops (demo_city's days 6–8 broke share went 11.4–12.9% against its 11% line). **Malls:** a shop chain's store whose merged plots reach 1,100 m² rebuilds as a mall (`ShopGrowth::Mall` → `World::build_mall`), and a new commercial building on a plot that big is one half the time; its company (`firm_of`) owns it, its store is an anchor; jobs 2× a shop floor, 30 spaces a plot. **Mega-malls:** a mall its local company owns, open 7+ days with at most a quarter of its shops to let, buys the commercial plots round it through the land market (`plots_next_to`, up to 8 plots, 3,000 m²+) and rebuilds as a mega-mall (one a day at most, `grow_malls`): 2 anchors, 4 food-court counters, 12–28 shops, 60 spaces a plot; its shops draw people from 3× as far, a mall's 1.6× (`World::shop_reach`, in `choose_shop`). Drawn procedurally (`nd_procgen/src/buildings/malls.rs`, styles `StripMall` / `Mall` / `MegaMall` from the form): a strip mall's stucco block behind its car park with a covered walk, an upstairs gallery and stair, a sign over each door (lit in the tenant's neon, a pale board when to let) and a pylon sign of its tenants by the street; a mall's two-level block with a band of tenant signs per level, a glass entrance atrium and a car park round it; a mega-mall's anchor boxes with big signs, a glazed food-court roof, a three-deck parking structure (beside it on a wide site, behind it on a narrow one) and a pylon. Everything from the building's seed, so the same from every camera. Remote: `inspect.building.land.form` strip_mall / mall / mega_mall (docs/REMOTE.md). Measured (`examples/mall_probe.rs` on a copy of Harvey's day-61 autosave, 7 days; main has none): strip malls 0 → 4 (3 still going up on day 68; the open one 1 of 3 let), malls 0, mega-malls 0 (his commercial land is built up: 29 of 187 road plots fit a strip mall, 13 a mall, none vacant); ledger sum 0 every day; unemployment days 62–68 main 3.4/1.7/1.3/0.6/0.3/0.7/0.5% → 3.4/1.7/0.6/0.6/0.4/0.7/0.5%; population day 68 15,436 → 14,979 (another run of this branch 14,838; an earlier version was 15,698 on day 67 against main's 15,530): not judged, worth a look → judged [p1-sim-leftovers]: not caused by the malls or land assembly. Same save, same probe (`ledger_probe`, 7 days to day 68), each run deterministic: 4151aa0 (before G2) 15,436; main with G2 14,979; main with strip-mall rebuilds, mall growth, new malls and site assembly all switched off 14,717 (lower still); rebuilds off alone part from main on day 64 and run below it. Every version loses ~5,000 people in the week to hunger (2,200–3,300 hungry at a time, 22 → 57 grocers open), and the day-to-day toll moves by a few hundred with any change to the order things happen (G2's other edits: unit roles, shop reach, zoning), so the 3% is the spread of that exodus, not malls pulling down homes (no homes are pulled down by them; homes 7,637 → 8,092 in the G2 run). The hunger exodus itself is a new item below. Test save (`mall_probe --scene --force-mega`): the biggest shop site made a mall and grown into a mega-mall at once (18 shops, 8 let after 4 days, 5 tenants), a forced mall 6 of 6 let, three strip malls opened (1–2 of 3 let each). Screenshots O:/nd_targets/g2-land-malls/run1/screenshots (strip_mall, strip_mall2, mall, mega_mall). demo_sweep 1 16 (main re-run in this target: day 8 15.9%, days 18–20 9.5%) → 15.9% / 8.1%. Left: the forced mega-mall stands on a bike shop's long narrow forecourt, so it looks cramped (small anchors); in a built-up city malls come slowly (rebuilds only) and none grew into a mega-mall on its own in 7 days; there is no "mall" business kind (the main store is an ordinary shop); the inspector shows only the form's label (no mall summary).
  - → DONE [p2-look-leftovers] (G2's leftovers, looks and inspector): **Cramped mega-mall:** cause: a forced mega-mall bought its block at a looser hull slack (1.35) than `World::footprint` joins it at (`MALL_HULL_SLACK` 1.12), so the footprint fell back to the road plot alone (the bike shop's 22 m wide forecourt) and the whole mega-mall was squeezed onto it. Now the finished block must join at the slack it is drawn at (on the way it may be an L, `MEGA_MALL_GROWING_SLACK` 1.35), plots are bought towards a square block (each the one bringing its sides nearest a square of the size wanted; on small plots taking the squarest step each turn ran along the street into a strip), else, if that block will not join, the squarest step each turn at the drawn slack (`mega_mall_plots_by`), and a site whose short side is under 45% of its long side or under 36 m is refused (`MEGA_MALL_MIN_ASPECT`, `MEGA_MALL_MIN_SIDE`, `World::site_extent` / `mega_mall_shape_ok`). On the test save the forced one is now Starving Artists Co-op on 8 plots, 70 × 55 m (was Red Cycles on one 22 × 83 m plot). **Massing** (`nd_procgen/src/buildings/malls.rs`): malls and mega-malls are now halls with pilasters, a lit sign band per level and shop windows, a glazed skylight ridge, anchor stores as their own taller blocks (panel, brick or stucco by seed, a reveal band, a big lit letter sign or a pale to-let board, a deep entrance canopy with display windows, a loading dock at the back, rooftop plant), a mullioned glass entrance atrium with the mall's sign, the food court as a glass pavilion on the upper level on columns (the counters' signs glowing inside; a glazed roof behind it on a mega-mall), rooftop plant (air handlers, ducts, a stack), a car park with planted islands and trees, and on a mega-mall a 3-deck parking structure with a ramp, stair cores, under-deck strip lights, a P sign and cars on every deck. A mall's own store is its anchor (at one end, by seed) when the block is 30 m+ wide. **Strip malls:** the signs were under the covered walk's roof, hidden from every camera above; each shop's sign is now on a fascia along the walk's edge (lit letters in the tenant's colour, a pale board to let) with a small board over each door; the block is shallow (8–14 m) with the car park taking the rest (a deep plot had an 18 m deep box behind one row of bays); bay lines are painted (they were a faint glow, invisible by day). **Inspector** (`ui/inspector/site.rs`): a STRIP MALL / MALL / MEGA-MALL section near the top: shops, anchors, food-court counters, let / to let, tenants, the owner (`ui::estate::owner_text`), car park or parking structure with spaces and in use, shopper reach (`World::shopper_reach_m`: 450 m × the mall's pull, e.g. 1.4 km, 3.0× a street shop), plots and area, then who has each anchor, counter and shop (tenants clickable) and the offices upstairs; the plain UNITS grid is left out for malls. - [x] District stories instead of loose dials (Harvey 2026-10-15). Today a district has separate dials (crates/nd_sim/src/districts.rs `District`): homes look (Kowloon stacks / Suburban / Eco / Capsule), shops look (Neon strip / Tech campus / Night market / Tourist quarter / Capsule), industry sub-zone (an IndustryKind), Historic freeze, height limit; no district taxes or policies yet. Make each district pick a **story**: a named preset with a short backstory that fills in the homes and shops architecture, industry, height, historic freeze and its own policies (district taxes and levers, see the district tax laws item). The player can fine-tune the policy dials, but not the building types without switching the story. Each story gets its own unique architecture over time (Capsule exists; the rest reuse today's looks until they get their own). Starting set (Harvey approved the idea; the table shown to him on 2026-10-15): Capsule (an eccentric inventor's land grant, domes inspired by a ship that came down in the hills: no Dragon Ball names), Eco Village (futuristic homes with green and solar roofs), The Walled Quarter (Kowloon), Afterglow Mile (neon strip nightlife), Founders' Campus (tech), Old Town Charter (historic), Quiet Acres (suburb), The Promenade (tourist/harbour), Foundry Row (industry), Lantern Lanes (night market).
  - → DONE [g3-stories]: `crates/nd_sim/src/stories.rs`: 12 stories (the ten above plus Riverbank Shantytown and Neo Tokyo Heights), each with a backstory, the looks/industry/Historic/height it sets and its own starting policies. `District::story` (+ `policies`, `books`); picking a story (`PolicyChange::story`) fills in the building-type dials, height limit and policies; the player fine-tunes the policy dials and height limit only (setting a building type by hand through the API makes it "Custom"). **The story → look mapping is one function, `Story::preset`**: when a story gets its own look (Round 4), add it as a `ResidentialSpec`/`CommercialSpec` variant (appended, as Capsule) and change that story's line there; `Story::planned_look` names the look each is waiting for. Today: Capsule = Capsule/Capsule; Eco Village = Eco homes + Farming industry, 40 m; Walled Quarter = Kowloon + Night market; Afterglow Mile = Neon strip shops; Founders' Campus = Eco + Tech campus; Old Town Charter = Historic, 24 m; Quiet Acres = Suburban, 18 m; The Promenade = Eco + Tourist quarter, 60 m; Foundry Row = Manufacturing; Lantern Lanes = Night market shops; Shantytown = Kowloon homes, 12 m; Neo Tokyo Heights = Eco + Neon strip. Old saves (`Districts::adopt_stories` on load): a district whose dials are exactly a story's takes it (height limit kept, the story's policies); others stay "Custom" (the panel offers "Switch to <closest story>"); no dials = "No story". UI: City Data, City, Districts: story picker (combo with backstory on hover, plus a table of every story, its backstory and what it sets), policy dials, height limit, and per district the policy money yesterday, today and the growth pull; the District tool's pane has the same editor. Remote: `district {action: "stories"}`, `district set {story, rent_support, ...}`, story/policies/books in `district list` (docs/REMOTE.md). The Perfect AI City's districts now pick stories (`citygen/furnish.rs`; Midtown lost its Kowloon homes, West Farms is an Eco Village). Left: the new looks and per-story mansions below (Round 4).
