- [x] Companies grow from local roots (Harvey 2026-10-15). What his day-62 city shows (read from the live game): the biggest owners are five out-of-town developers (Meridian Estates 60 properties, Kessler Land 52, Marlowe Holdings 51, Argent Holdings 46, Halcyon Developments 41; the fixed list `DEVELOPERS` in world/estate/mod.rs plus `towers::developer_name` for the tower league) and the university (33); the biggest local owner has 6. "Biggest companies" in City Data (ui/estate.rs, `World::companies` in world/corp.rs) counts only Firm/Person owners and only their open businesses, ranked by takings, so developers, the university and rent never show and a one-venue owner tops the list.
  1. **Company directory with search:** every owner (developers, firms, founders, corps, university, billionaires) with properties, value, rent and takings, staff, vehicles and history; a search box on every directory page.
  2. **Fix "Biggest companies":** rank by size (properties and value, rent included), include every kind of owner, and say what each one is.
  3. **Local companies only:** no built-in out-of-town developers or investment firms. Every company starts in town, founded by a resident with one building; housing developers become local builders/landlord firms that grow. Companies can grow, rename and merge; mega-corp rules (share cap, antitrust levy) stay.
  4. **Fleet companies with colours:** every local company with vehicles (taxis, trucking, tow trucks, construction, delivery) has its own livery colour. It starts with one building and a few vehicles, expands its plot into a lot for more vehicles, buys more plots, runs more vehicles and wins more calls (more of its colour on the streets); rivals start in different parts of town and compete for reach.
  5. **Shops grow into corporations:** a general store that does well buys adjacent land, builds a mall, and can become a mega-corp.
  6. **Specialty from its roots:** every mega-corp has a specialty that comes from where it started and steers what it builds next: consumer products → shipping, depots, delivery vans and couriers for packages (Amazon-like); cars → dealerships, car lots, big car factories and parking lots; groceries → mega-farms, huge warehouse distribution centres and many grocery stores around town; and so on for other roots.
  - Ties in with the land-assembly item below (companies buy the land behind their road plots to grow).
  - → DONE [g1-companies] items 1–6 (`crate::companies`, `world/companies.rs`, tests `world/companies_tests.rs`):
    1. **Directory:** City Data › Directory › **Companies** (new page between Businesses and People): every owner (builders, firms, founders, property companies, mega-corps with their specialty, the university, billionaires, families, old developers), with founder, properties, value, rent and takings yesterday, staff, vehicles, since, and its latest history line (each owner now keeps a history: founded, bought from, grew, renamed, merged, sold up); search over names, founders, kinds and the whole history; pickers for kind of owner and district; name in its fleet colour; click selects its HQ/biggest building (the map outlines all it owns). Businesses and People already had a search box. Remote: `directory {kind: companies, owner_kind, ...}` (docs/REMOTE.md).
    2. **Biggest companies** (Economy › Corporations) now lists every kind of owner (families in their own house left out) by size = what its buildings would sell for + a year of its rent and takings (`World::biggest_companies` / `company_summaries`), each saying what it is and who founded it, with a show button. Corp cards show the specialty.
    3. **Local only:** `DEVELOPERS` and `towers::developer_name` are gone. Densify, lot assembly and foreclosure buy through `World::builder_for`: a local builder and landlord (`OwnerKind::Builder { founder }`), the nearest by its roots (bigger firms count as further off so rivals get room), or a new one founded by the resident with the most to put in near the site (one family founds one firm), named after them ("Okafor Builders"), up to 1 per 1,500 people (max 12). Builders collect rent like a property company (upkeep, tax, mortgage, half the net to the founder), pay their site materials, rename past 8 buildings ("… Development Group"), and a big one buys out a small rival nearby (≤2 buildings, ≥4× its size, books and all). Tower-league developers are local firms named after a resident ("Nakamura Tower Group"). Mega-corp rules unchanged; builders and property companies may now grow into one. **Old saves:** developers stop buying and sell 10 buildings a day each (oldest first, at the sale price, money from the bank as every sale) to local builders until they own nothing and leave; an old tower developer's record sells up the same way and the league firm gets a new local record (test `an_old_saves_developers_sell_up_to_local_builders`, built from a save with the new fields stripped).
    4. **Fleet liveries:** `Livery::Fleet(trade, colour)`: every local company with vehicles (cabs, tow trucks, garbage trucks, trucking yards, shops' and makers' vans, a builder's site lorries) gets a colour from 16 (`FLEET_COLOURS`) no rival of the same trade within 2.5 km wears, on its owner record or its business; drawn as the trade's livery (cab sign and checker, site lorry beacon) in that colour (nd_procgen vehicles.rs). Tow, trash and trucking yards now buy the plot next door first (up to 2 plots, more staff, more trucks) then open depots across town; new tow/trash yards are marked down within 700 m of a rival so they start in different parts of town. Taxi firms already grew cabs and branches.
    5. **Shops into corps:** a grocery or gadget shop doing well buys next door (up to 4 plots), then opens more stores across town (`World::grow_firm`, `shop_growth`); the chain counts towards mega-corp promotion. Hook for G2: `ShopGrowth::Mall` (never returned yet).
    6. **Specialty:** `Specialty::of_origin` (goods → depots, container yards, assembly; cars → dealerships, repair, car factories, tow; groceries → farms, distribution yards, groceries; hospitality; energy; heavy industry; offices/homes). A corp builds its specialty's kinds on land it buys (if the plot is big enough) and counts them as nearer when buying.
    - Measured (company_probe, copy of Harvey's day-62 autosave, 3 days, main → g1): owners by kind day 64: Developer 5/250 → 5/92 (selling up), Builder 0 → 12/171; top owner Meridian Estates 60 → the university 33, then Haddad Development Group 28, Kowalski 21, Achebe, Volkov 19…; local companies 60 → 75; fleets: same 9 (taxis 6/2, tow 0–2 each); liveries on the road: 7 kinds → 24 (17 company colours); ledger sum 0 every day; unemployment 3.4/1.2/0.7% → 3.7/1.5/1.4% (pop 16.8k, days 62–64). demo_sweep 1 16: day 8 15.2% → 15.9%, days 18–20 8.5% → 9.5%; the demo never reaches a builder purchase or a shop expansion in 20 days (switching the builders' mortgage off or the shop change gave identical numbers), so the per-seed spread is the changed draw order (seeds move ±3–9 points both ways). Screenshots: O:/nd_targets/g1-companies/run2/screenshots (directory_companies, biggest_companies, fleet_trucks, fleet_cab3).
    - Left: malls (G2 via `ShopGrowth::Mall`); joined backland sites (Zone any parcel); the independent landlords' pool (unowned buildings' rent) still sends its surplus out of town; builders' "vehicles" count only lorries from their own businesses (site lorries come in from the edge); cabs show as "passing through" in the car inspector's label.
    - → [p1-sim-leftovers] Money gaps from Round 2/3: **wages** are paid employer → worker: a business pays each of its staff's households their payroll wage (60% of the job's wage, what its payroll always was; `PAYROLL_SHARE`, `World::wage_payer`) out of its own account in the household's daily pass (`wages_and_pensions`, businesses → households, noted in `wages_paid`), and the market's daily books send out of town only the payroll of staff not paid that way (no household); the rest of a worker's pay (skill, school, the wage over the payroll) and everyone else's (city jobs, building sites, gigs, pensions) still comes in from outside. A business's day costs exactly what it did, so no new failures. **Independent landlords:** what the pool holds over its $20,000 float, up to the last day's rent after upkeep and tax, is paid to the town's households with $5,000+ saved, in proportion to their savings (`LANDLORD_SAVINGS`), none of it out of town; past that it is the price of buildings the pool sold (the buyer's bank loan, from outside), which goes back out as a property sale, so a sale brings no money into town (paying it to the savers too handed them ~$2M a day on days 66–67 of his city while the builders bought up unowned buildings). Measured on Harvey's day-62 save, 7 days, main → this branch: population, unemployment, open/broke/closed businesses, homes and the treasury identical every day (14,979 on day 68, closed_total 84, treasury $4,048,279: the same money, now booked from the employer); payroll out of town $150–175k → $0 a day (business → household wages instead), landlords' profits out of town $84–100k a day → $0, to savers instead (households hold $29.6M on day 68 against $29.1M); ledger sum 0 every day, zero untracked, and with the per-tick check (`ND_LEDGER_CHECK=1`, 2 days) no drifts. demo_sweep 1 16: identical to main on every seed (day 8 15.9%, days 18–20 8.1%, 27.4 businesses closed per seed in 20 days). At the full wage it would not work yet: on his day-62 books the businesses that pay their staff take $350–390k a day and spend $230–270k (payroll $150–175k of it) while their staff earn $280–330k; paying that in full, 93–125 of the ~500 would lose money on the day, every day (the probe's `businesses' day` line), so `PAYROLL_SHARE` stays at the old payroll. Tests `ledger_tests::a_business_pays_its_staff_and_its_payroll_stays_in_town`, `the_independent_landlords_pay_their_surplus_to_savers_in_town`; `sim_checks` ledger test unchanged and green. Left: about half of a business worker's pay (and all of everyone else's) still comes from outside: paying it in full needs prices/takings to rise with it; the city's own staff are not paid by the treasury; income tax is still the treasury↔outside door, not withheld by employers.
- [x] Zone any parcel; companies assemble land behind the road (Harvey 2026-10-15). Any parcel can be zoned, including backland with no road frontage (today the Zone tool refuses landlocked backland, fix/roads-tools G5; lots carry `landlocked`, and placement allows backland only for some rules, world/placement.rs ~65–94). Building is up to the businesses: a zoned parcel can only be built on if it touches a road, or touches a parcel whose building is owned by the same company. A company owning a road-front parcel can buy up connected parcels behind it and join them into one bigger site:
  - **Strip mines:** expand into the backland behind their road parcel into big, realistic pits. Raise the strip mine's minimum plot size a little (IndustryKind::Mining → BusinessKind::StripMine, industry.rs).
  - **Most industry:** e.g. one company buying lots of land for a huge oil field.
  - **Car parks:** a company's parking lot can grow outward over parcels it buys behind its road plot (Harvey likes the massive company car park he saw).
  - Reuse the joined-site machinery that already exists (attractions/stadiums join plots via `plot.extra_lots`; `assemble_tower_site` in world/towers.rs; corp land in world/corp.rs, estate.rs) and the land market for buying neighbours. Drawn as one site, with access through the road-front parcel.
  - → DONE [g2-land-malls] (`world/land_assembly.rs`): the Zone tool's brush zones any parcel that is not rough or too steep, backland of any use included (`Parcel::refusal`; `parcel_refusal` no longer says "no parcel in front"); backland no lot in front takes in becomes a landlocked lot of its own and stays one through rebuilds (G5 left it unzoned and took it out of the stroke; `backland_tests` and the brush test rewritten for the new rule). Zones drawn as an area (a polygon, the city generator) still take backland only for the deep-lot uses (`Parcel::accepts_in_area`), so generated cities and old polygon zones are as before (the citygen landlocked-share test caught that). Nothing builds on a landlocked lot alone (`SiteRule::STREET`); it is built on only when joined to a road plot whose building owns it (`plots_next_to` wants a plot touching the site). **Open-ground trades grow in place** (`estate::grows_in_place`: strip mine, oil well, grain farm, dairy, cow and chicken farms, feedlot, surface car park): up to 2 plots at a time touching their site, empty ones first (backland first), then low buildings (2 floors at most, pulled down; a car park takes empty ground only), up to 8 plots (`BIG_SITE_LOTS`), staying open; empty land is bought at its land value with a 10% deposit from the business (to out of town; the bank lends the rest). A daily pass (`assemble_sites`, hash-picked, 2 a day) takes those open a week, staffed, not losing money and whose trade covers its costs, besides G1's `expand_firms`. The site is drawn as the union of its plots (`World::footprint`, hull slack 1.4), framed and entered by its road plot; a car park has the bays its whole site fits (on load too). **Most industry** (`expansion_form`: every industrial kind) rebuilds over its neighbours up to 6 plots (`INDUSTRY_SITE_LOTS`); a multi-storey car park does not. **Strip mines:** need a 1,500 m² plot (`industry::STRIP_MINE_MIN_AREA`; mining's weight is 0 on smaller ones); the pit gets a bench more every 10 m of radius past 18 m, a rim up to 2.5× higher and more haul trucks. Measured on Harvey's save (7 days): his city has no strip mine and only 9 empty landlocked lots (the tool refused backland until now), so natural growth was small: a grain farm 4 → 5 plots, 1 plot bought (main: 0). Test save (`mall_probe --scene`, backland behind a road plot zoned as the brush would): a strip mine bought 3 plots (1 backland) → 4 plots, 2,465 m², 32 jobs; a car park bought 4 backland plots → 5 plots, 2,622 m², 93 bays. Screenshots strip_mine_pit, grown_car_park (same folder). Tests `world/land_malls_tests.rs` (a strip mine digs into the backland, no mine on a small plot, a car park grows and keeps its bays on reload, a strip mall's layout, mall and mega-mall layouts, a thriving mall becomes a mega-mall, an old save loads), the ledger exact in each. Left: in a built-up city growth waits for the player to zone backland; the pit sits on a raised spoil rim (no cut into the terrain); a company's road plots elsewhere are not joined (only plots touching the site); an empty landlocked lot is drawn as vacant ground.
  - → DONE [p2-look-leftovers]: **The pit is dug into the ground.** Cause: the terrain is one height field drawn under every lot, so the old pit was built upward from it (benches stepping down from a 4–20 m spoil rim to ground level). Now the ground is cut: `nd_procgen::holes` cuts convex holes out of a ground mesh exactly (every triangle a hole touches split into its pieces outside it, corners interpolated on the triangle, so the ground keeps its shape to the edge); the landscape's tiles (`LandscapeTiles`, rebuilding only the tiles under a pit that opened, grew or closed), `build_landscape_terrain` and the flat map's terrain (`render/ground.rs`) all have every open strip mine's pit cut out (`holes::ground_holes`, keyed cheaply by `holes::holes_key`), and the lot gravel is left off a mine's plots (`holes::holed_lots`, `render/lots.rs`). The pit (`rural.rs`): the biggest ellipse that fits the site behind a strip kept along the street (searched over a few centres, a joined site's outline being ragged; `strip_mine_hole` gives the same edge to the renderer), a bench every 8 m of radius (+1 at the high tier, 2–8), steep rock faces (~67°) down to a floor at about half the radius deep (≈10 m on an 18 m pit, ≈18 m on a 35 m one) with standing water, the yard round it cut to the edge, a haul road as a ledge along the foot of each face rising a level per quarter turn and spiralling out to the street side, a dirt lane on to the road, a fence round the edge (open at the road), haul trucks on the floor, the benches when wide enough and at the top of the road, the conveyor from the floor over the edge to the ore heap (its legs standing on the bench under them), terraced spoil heaps on the front strip and in the free corners, the cabin. No rim: nothing of the pit stands above ground. Checked on the test save (Rust Mining, 4 plots, 2,465 m²) from high and low angles (after2_mine_high, _low, _low2, _low3; before_mine_*). Tests: `holes::tests::a_hole_cut_out_of_the_ground_leaves_the_rest_exact`, `economy_tests::a_strip_mine_pit_is_dug_below_the_ground`. The procgen sweeps' and `geometry_stays_near_its_lot`'s "below ground" check now exempts the one style that digs (`Style::digs`, StripMine only): the intended change; nothing else was loosened. **Inspector:** a SITE section for a business grown over the plots next door (mine, oil field, farm, car park, works): plots joined, backland among them, area, bays for a car park, the owner, the pit's size for a mine, how many plots it may grow to. Left: the pit is drawn at the lot's pad height (a mine on sloping ground digs down from its levelled pad); the conveyor and haul trucks do not move; a mine under construction or being demolished shows no hole.

  - **Places to eat and go out** (Harvey 2026-10-27: today commercial grows only grocery/bakery/gadget shop/office and entertainment only the generic club `Venue`; the only restaurants are seafood and pier restaurants on the waterfront). Add, each with its own look, sign, hours, staff and customers: diner, noodle/ramen bar, pizza place, fast-food joint with a drive-through, food hall, street-food stalls and food trucks, fine-dining restaurant, bubble-tea/juice bar; bar/pub, nightclub (today's Venue), karaoke box, cinema, theatre, concert hall/live-music venue, bowling alley, arcade/VR arcade, gym, comedy club.
  - **Where they go: commercial sub-zones (Harvey decided 2026-10-27: "the leisure sub-zone is good then the city can sort itself out, it was confusing for me to have those two levers").** Commercial gets a sub-zone row in the Zone tool like Industrial's (`INDUSTRY_CHOICES`, ui/tools.rs `zone`): **auto** (default; the city picks from the street, nearby homes, land value, demand and the district story), **neighbourhood** (corner groceries, bakeries, cafés, diners, noodle bars, small shops; low, near homes), **retail** (strip malls, malls, big shops, car dealers, drive-through fast food; main roads), **offices** (office buildings and towers with ground-floor cafés), **leisure** (restaurants, bars, clubs, cinemas, theatres, bowling, karaoke). On auto: quiet streets by homes → neighbourhood, busy avenues → retail, dear downtown → offices, nightlife stories (Afterglow Mile, Lantern Lanes) lean leisure. **Remove the separate Entertainment zone** from the Build window and Zone tool: old saves' entertainment lots become commercial with the leisure sub-zone (clubs keep working; demand, taxes (nightlife lines), land value, names, trash rates, district story weights, the zoning layer colour, the remote API `land_use: entertainment` (accept it as an alias) all follow). Real-world basis: US zoning splits commercial into neighbourhood / general / regional plus offices, with nightlife by permit or district overlay; Cities: Skylines has commercial specialisations (Leisure, Tourism) and a separate office zone. → DONE [r4-leisure]: see the summary below.
    - DONE [r4-leisure]: `nd_sim::commercial` (`CommercialSub`, `COMMERCIAL_CHOICES`) and `world/commercial.rs`. The Zone tool shows "sub-zone: Auto (city decides) / Neighbourhood / Retail / Offices / Leisure" under Commercial (with a note per choice and, over a parcel, the sub-zone set or what the city would build there now); the Build window and Zone tool no longer offer Entertainment. Leisure land keeps the old `LandUse::Entertainment` value inside the sim (label "Leisure", `zoned_as()` Commercial), so clubs, nights out, nightlife taxes, trash, land value, names, building styles and story pulls carry on unchanged and old saves need no rewrite: their entertainment lots simply read as commercial · leisure. Neighbourhood/retail/offices are a bias over the ground like industry's (`World::commercial_bias`, serde default; painted with the stroke, applied on implement). Each maps onto today's kinds: neighbourhood grocery/bakery/gadget shop (cafés, bookshops as tenants), retail gadget shop/grocery (car dealers, bike shops by pressure), offices office, leisure venue (driving range, beach bar, café by pressure). On auto the city picks per lot, with no random draw (`AutoSite::pick`): homes within 160 m → neighbourhood, 3+ lane streets → retail, dear land → offices, a nightlife story or late licence (Afterglow Mile, Lantern Lanes) or a town much hungrier for nights out than shops → leisure (only while venues would pay and never while the town is short of grocers); a town short of work leans to offices; a leisure pick builds a club on leisure land use with the lot staying commercial, an offices pick an office, and a neighbourhood or retail pick (much the same kinds today) lets the market choose from the whole commercial menu as before. Over the demo's seeds 1-8 (`demo_sweep`) unemployment days 18-20 averaged 8.9% against 10.0% with auto off (day 8: 15.0% both). Lots set to a sub-zone wait while the market wants none of its kinds. Demand window/City Data: commercial with its sub-zone bars; taxes page "Commercial · leisure (nightlife)"; zoning layer: leisure in a violet-blue of commercial's family; map legend "Commercial · leisure". Remote: `land_use: leisure` (alias `entertainment`, `nightlife`), `sub_zone` on `paint`, `add_zone`, `tool`; lot rows `zoned_as`, `sub_zone` (+ `sub_zone_auto_pick` in a lot's detail); `report` overview `commercial_demand`; land-use keys say `leisure` for the old `entertainment`. Tests: old entertainment save migration, the alias and painting, auto pick (unit and in a world). On a copy of Harvey's day-63 save (14,110 people): 376 commercial and 190 entertainment lots load as 566 commercial lots, 190 of them leisure; 62 leisure buildings with 50 venues (39 open, 452 staff); run on two days (`leisure_probe`): venues 50 → 55 (43 open, 550 staff), leisure buildings 62 → 67, 2,722-2,732 people out or at a leisure place at 22:00, nightlife tax $5.5k → $5.9k a day; Demand: shops/offices 71-73%, leisure 90%, neighbourhood and retail 0% (the market would open no new grocer, bakery or gadget shop). Also fixed on the way: a car dropping a child where its next stop lay behind it on the same lane kept timing the trip from setting off (`drop_off` now starts the new leg in that case too). Left: the new restaurant/cinema/etc. kinds (the list above) so the sub-zones have more to grow; a comparison run against main over several seeds.
- [x] TOP: Several businesses in one building; mall bases in supertalls (Harvey 2026-10-15: "buildings definitely need to have more than one business, that's kinda crazy for this simulation"). Today `Building::business` is an `Option<Business>` (crates/nd_sim/src/buildings.rs), so every building holds at most one business. Make it a list: ground-floor shops under flats and offices, several offices per tower floor range, mixed-use blocks, strip malls and malls (the malls item). Each tenant has its own kind, staff, books, hours, rent to the building's owner and sign on the facade; vacancy per unit; the building's owner collects rents. Touches the market, jobs, deliveries, rent, the inspector (a tenant list), the directory, the economy panels and saves (old saves: the single business becomes the only tenant). Prerequisite for strip malls, malls and mega-malls.
  - The simulation sorts out who occupies what by size, no fixed rules: a small firm rents one unit; a growing company takes more floors or units; a mega-corp can buy or build a huge building for itself and rent out the ground-floor shops (or not). How much space a company needs follows its staff and business; how much a building offers follows its floors and footprint; owners fill empty units from the market.
  - → DONE [round2/e2-tenants]: a building that can be let in parts is cut into **units** when it opens (`Building::units`, `World::plan_units`, `world/tenants.rs`): shopfronts along its street front (one per 14 m, up to 4) under flats of 3+ floors and in shop/office buildings of 2+ floors, upper floors in ranges (one a floor in a low block, up to 8 ranges in a tower), a two-storey mall base of 4–10 shops in a supertall. Each unit has its floors, floor area (plot × 55% cover), jobs (an office block's jobs spread by area, so the total is as before; a shop under flats at 1.5× a shop floor's staffing) and an occupant: empty, the building's own business (`business`, kept so the ~500 readers and old saves still work) or a **tenant**: a record of its own in `World::buildings` with `unit_of` its host, so it hires, sells, orders, is delivered to, robbed, listed and keeps its own account (`business <id>`), hours and books like any business, but stands on the host's lot, is drawn as the host (`render::building_chunk`, the StyleBook skip it), is special (never bought, upgraded or redeveloped) and goes when the host goes (`evict_tenants` from `vacate`/`demolish_with`; they pack up to move first). Who has what follows size and kind, no fixed list: a shop keeps the street and floors up to 24 jobs (the rest let as offices), anything else the floors (the shops under it let); a corp or firm running its own building keeps its street front or lets it (by seed). Owners let empty units daily (`let_units`, up to 8 a day, hash picks, no RNG draws): a shopfront to a shop kind the market would open (`kinds_viable`, cafés/bookshops on dear streets), more readily with commercial demand; upper floors to offices while the city is short of jobs. A business fully staffed whose trade pays (cover ≥ 1.05) takes the empty unit next door (`grow_into_units`); a shut tenant leaves after 3 days and its unit is to let; a building's own business shutting while others rent there leaves its units empty instead of the building being pulled down (`vacate_main`); a bought-out business moves into an empty unit nearby before building anew (`relocate.rs`, open at once, staff back). Rent (`shop_rent`): floor area × 12% of a home's rent here per 70 m² (so tier, land value, condition), at most half the day's profit, to the host's owner (tenants always pay). Old saves: every building is cut on load, its business keeping units for all its staff (nobody laid off); Harvey's day-61 save: 892 units, 345 to let. Shown: inspector UNITS section (each unit, its tenant (click), to let N days), a tenant's "Rents ground floor of X"; remote `inspect` `tenants`/`units`/`units_vacant`/`tenant_of` (old `business` kept: own, or first tenant), `list businesses` with `tenant_of`, `list buildings` structures only (docs/REMOTE.md); directory search finds tenants by building; Market KPIs (tenants, units to let, let/taken); procgen: a sign over each shopfront, lit in the tenant's neon, a pale board when to let (`buildings/shopfronts.rs`, placed off the drawn ground floor so any style). Measured (`ledger_probe` on a copy of Harvey's save, 3 days, main → e2): open businesses 476/474/474 → 485/493/504, jobs 9859/9986/10097 → 9818/10166/10372, unemployment 3.4/3.0/1.8% → 3.4/1.2/0.7%, units to let 345 → 330 (25 tenants), broke 16/14/13 → 21/20/18, shut 14/11/10 → 14/11/9, treasury 3.57/3.81/3.95M → 3.61/3.83/3.99M, rent-stressed 565/632/721 → 568/635/686, homeless 2275/2164/2428 → 2224/2197/2418, shop rent ~$7.2k → ~$8.8k a day, ledger sum 0 every day and zero drift under the per-tick check, tick ~77–117 s → 76–88 s a game day (runs side by side, noisy), save 46.5 → 45.4 MB gz (different state). demo_sweep 1 16: day 8 19.2% → 15.2%, days 18–20 8.8% → 8.5% (seed 5 29.3% → 5.4%; seeds 2, 6, 9, 13, 14 worse by 3–7 points); with no shop rent at all 15.2% / 8.9%, so the late figure is now seed noise more than rent. Left: strip malls/malls (G2); `World::companies` still ranks owners' own businesses only (tenants skipped); growth into units is rare (none in 3 days on Harvey's save); shop kinds for shopfronts are the commercial menu only (new kinds item).
  - → DONE [round2/e2-tenants] Money flow (E1 follow-ups): (a) band labels spread so none overlaps or leaves the chart (`spread_labels`, test); (b) the $1.7M outside → landlords band on the demo was one resident buying a block at its full estate value (`sale_price`, ~800 days of its rent) with a bank loan: real, once that day, not booked daily. The real artefact was elsewhere: a developer rebuilding its own block (or a firm taking its own plot) was paid the full price from out of town by itself (`settle_sale_to` now moves nothing then): on Harvey's save outside → companies "property sales" 4.25/2.96M → 4.03/1.54M a day; the rest is developers buying each other's blocks to rebuild. Property sales now have their own group on the page ("sales") so a day's sale no longer flattens the rent beside it.

- [x] District tax laws and levers (e.g. a student district) → DONE [g3-stories]: `stories::Policies`, set by a district's story and fine-tuned per district, all money through the E1 door (`world/stories.rs`; treasury rows "district levies"/"district spending", itemised "<district>: <policy>"; the district's own books by policy): **rent support** (city pays back a share of residents' rent; rent stress and `choose_home` read the net rent, so supported homes draw tenants), **noise & alcohol levy** (share of venue/bar takings), **late licence** (venues and bars open 2 h later, `Business::late_hours`/`Hours::later`; residents −0.01 mood a night), **pollution levy** (share of industry takings; the district's dirty plants foul up to 2× the levy less), **heritage grant** ($/building/day to owners; wear cut up to 75%), **business rate** (levy or relief on shops' and offices' takings), **bed levy** (on hotel nights). Levies are a cost on the business's cover like a tax over the standard; growth in the district follows `Policies::pull` (support/relief draw builders, levies put them off, ×0.5–1.5). Nothing changes in a city with no policy (test). Left: tuition support goes through rent (no per-student tuition hook yet); no per-district income tax. Measured (`stories_probe` on a copy of Harvey's day-61 autosave, 3 days; base = every district's policies cleared, which runs as main did: unemployment 3.4/1.2/0.7%, treasury 3.61/3.83/3.99M, same as E2's numbers). On load his districts became Founders' Campus (Jade Terraces), Afterglow Mile (Iron Flats), Capsule (Neon Heights), Quiet Acres (Iron Commons); Signal Wharf (Kowloon + Tourist) stays Custom (suggests Riverbank Shantytown), Static Gardens No story. With those plus Signal Wharf → The Promenade and Static Gardens → Old Town Charter: district money a day ≈ −$24–28k rent support in Jade Terraces (15%), −$3.6k heritage grants, −$0.5k business-rate relief, +$1.0–1.5k nightlife levy in Iron Flats, +$0.14k bed levy; net about −$31k/day out of ~+$200k/day income (ledger sum 0, untracked 0 every day). Jade Terraces' residents 5,396/5,333/5,323 → 5,779/5,862/5,983 (rent support draws tenants into its free homes), Static Gardens' rent-stressed households 190/223/240 → 151/186/206, homeless 2,224/2,197/2,418 → 2,052/2,053/2,324; city unemployment 3.4/1.2/0.7% → 3.4/3.0/1.4% (one run each, not yet judged over seeds). At 25% rent support the same district cost ~$41–52k a day, so Founders' Campus starts at 15%.

I keep zoning more land it never goes down" → DONE [fix/civic-demand]: civic demand was `0.1 + pop/150` (100% past ~135 people, blind to what was built) and park demand a flat 50%. Both are now a supply gap (`Economy::civic_gap` / `park_gap`, `Shortfall`): need (civic offices 3 m² of floor a head, rising to 4 at 20k people; parks 6 m² a head) less what is open, what is going up (counted in full, and the light part of the Demand bar) and a quarter of what the vacant zoned lots could hold (at most half the shortfall: zoning shows, only building meets it); scaled by the old small-town ramp. Demand panel and zoning tool say why ("12122 people need about 43,713 m² of civic offices; the city has 179,047 m²"). Entertainment checked: not stuck, it follows venue takings (`cover`), and says so. Harvey's day-50 city: civic 100% → 5% (218 civic offices, ~179,000 m² of floor for 12,122 people, 4× the need), park 50% → 75% (26 parks, 16,836 m², 1.4 m² a head)

## I. City services
- [ ] Fusion power plant (Harvey 2026-10-15): a late-game, very expensive, clean, huge-output plant; a glowing sci-fi landmark (a ring or sphere with a blue-white core and light pulses), needs much land and staff, no smoke.
- [ ] Community centres (Harvey 2026-10-15): a placeable civic building (classes, youth club, seniors' lunches, meeting hall, sports court) that raises mood and ties for the people within reach and lowers petty crime locally; programmes and opening hours; staffed and with upkeep. Cemeteries later, once people age and die.
- [ ] Where the trash goes (Harvey 2026-10-15). Today a city depot's trucks unload at the depot and the trash simply disappears (crates/nd_sim/src/trash.rs: no landfill, no disposal cost, nothing toxic); only the private hauler "drives the trash out of town". Add, under Buildings > Services > trash:
  - **Landfill:** a placeable dump on its own land. It auto-expands onto adjacent lots the player zones or gives it as it fills, looks really gross (heaps, gulls, leachate, trucks tipping), makes the land around it toxic (the contamination/dirt system), lowers land value and mood nearby. Scavengers pick through it at night (homeless and poor residents, a little income for them, some risk).
  - **Default, no landfill or incinerator:** depots haul the trash out of town at a clearly higher cost per tonne (trucks drive it off the map edge, like the private hauler).
  - **Incinerator:** burns trash with no land to fill, but costs more to run and is more toxic (air pollution and contamination downwind, a smokestack).
  - Budget and City Data show tonnes collected, where they went (landfill / out of town / burned) and what each costs.
- [ ] "Trashy streets" map layer (Harvey 2026-10-15): in the View panel (City tab, likely People or Environment), colour streets and blocks by overflowing bins and litter on the pavement (trash.rs overflow, park litter), with landfills and incinerators marked.

## J. Flags 2026-10-11 night to 10-15 (docs/playtest/flags_2026-10-15.md, K#)
Not yet in the list (the stuck police cars, footbridge braking, the racer are fixed; Kade's dead-end loops and U-turns are in C).

**Road and path tools**
- [x] Adding a raised path raised the underground paths beneath it, and it can't be undone (K1) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: cause: a way crossing another a few metres off its level (in his day-3 save a path at grade over a pedestrian tunnel's shallow portal ramp, 4 m down; a path raised 2–4 m over a path or street) met it at a junction, the junction node taking one level, so the way under it was pulled up (or a deck dragged down). Such a crossing, or passing a junction of the other way within snapping reach, is now refused with the reason and the height that would do (`World::crossing_refusal`, `CROSSING_LEVEL_SLACK` 1 m): in the Road tool, `add_road` and on Implement (so the Edit tool too); a refused plan is discarded with nothing under it moved. Tests `world/r1_roads_tests.rs`
- [x] Intersection editor: the junction dot and the lane arrows overlap, so the dot can't be clicked (K2) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: the crosswalk dot wins under the pointer (`DOT_PICK`); the arrow's badge still answers on the rest of its face
- [x] A path up a mountainside levelled the hill and put the path on stilts; paths should follow the slope (K5) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: a path at grade was laid in 30 m chords between its inner height samples, so on a hillside it cut metres into every crest (the ground graded down to it) and stood over the hollows; paths are now sampled every 6 m (`PATH_INNER_STEP`) and lie on the slope (probe on his day-26 island save, a path up the hill at (1295,-56): within 0.3 m of the natural ground at every sample; before, 3.6 m under it at the crest). Left: the built ground beside it still dips up to ~2 m where hillside lot pads levelled to the ring street reach it (lot terracing, not the path)
- [x] A raised path at a height that conflicts with the roads under it should error and require going higher (K12) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: refused with the reason and the height needed: within 4.5 m of a way it would meet (it used to join it there), or less than 4.5 m over the highway (which meets nothing: its deck stood in the lorries), measured against the way's real height (an embankment counts)
- [x] Raising a path in Edit put a pillar in the middle of the highway: Edit must apply the build tool's pillar rules (K13) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: cause: the path raised to 4 m met the 3-lane street (Elm Way) at a junction at 4 m, lifting it, and a column site only keeps off ways more than 2 m under the deck, so one stood in its lanes. The Edit tool is held to the crossing rules on Implement (refused, as K12), and the pillar plan of an edited road leaves out the road as it was (it blocked every column of its own new deck: `road_pillar_plan_replacing`, the hologram too)
- [x] A footbridge at the end of a raised path should merge into it, stay raised and step down like the path (K14) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: a footbridge over the zebra where a raised path comes down to the street (his path onto Elm Way at (253,465)) joins the path: no stairs on the path's side, the path's deck runs on into the bridge (`Footbridge::path_join`, drawn by `nd_procgen::terrain::path_viaducts`), and its ramp down to the kerb is no longer walked; the far end steps down
- [x] Paths make crosswalks a few feet from an intersection's crosswalk: near a crossing or footbridge, let the path end on the street (K15) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: a path's zebra within 20 m of a junction crosswalk or another zebra over the same street is closed (not painted, not walked; `PATH_ZEBRA_NEAR`): the path ends on the pavement. Near a footbridge `FOOTBRIDGE_REPLACES` already closed it
- [x] Intersection editing on the road itself, with a box round the area and time paused until done (K20) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: no more modal over a filmed top-down view: the arrows and crosswalk dots are drawn on the roads through the map camera (`ui::junction::on_map`) inside a dashed box round the junction, the camera flies over it, the controls sit in a panel at the right, time stands still while it is open and goes on as it was after (`hold_time`), and the other tools leave the map alone; Esc or Done closes
- [x] Three car parks placed side by side: only two merged (K42) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: cause: City Parking 20 and 50 met along an edge that was not quite one line (a corner 0.3 m off it); the union left a sliver and they never joined. Plots are now put on one line within 1 m before joining (`join_plots`). Left: a plot touching two existing city lots joins the first only (those two lots are not merged into each other)

**Zoning and parcels**
- [x] Much of the land inside a circus is un-zoneable, "no road", though it reaches the road (K8) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: cause: land along a curve fronts it in short edges (7 m pieces, shorter on the inside of the bend) and the frontage test wanted one edge of 6 m or more; the run of road edges along the curve counts now (`frontage_long_enough`)
- [x] A parcel with 3 streets around it says it has none (K9) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: cause: the parcel at (606,-663) was a U of ground left round a lot kept inside it; its sharp inner corners made it no lot, which was reported as "no street". Such rests are split at their inner corners into pieces that each front their street (`split_at_corners`), and an awkward shape that does front a road says so (`Parcel::awkward`)

**Dead ends and crosswalks**
- [x] A dead-end road gets a sidewalk round its end, not a crosswalk; walkers walk round the end (K47, K48) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: the walk graph runs the pavement round a dead end's end (where `cul_de_sac` paves it) instead of an unmarked crossing over the turning head, so nobody stands in the road there
- [x] A footbridge didn't remove the crosswalk next to it (K48) → DONE [round1/r1-roads]: Willow Broadway is a 19 m 2+2 stub off Zenith Circus; his footbridge was over the dead end's own crossing, 4 m from the junction crosswalk over the stub's mouth. The dead end has no crossing now (pavement round it), and a junction crosswalk over a dead-end stub of 30 m or less is closed too (`DEAD_END_STUB`): walkers go round its end
- [ ] Two trucks stuck on a tiny dead-end road that leads nowhere: why route there, and why stuck (K47)

**Bus lines**
- [x] No bus stop allowed on a one-way exit ramp ("NO ROAD"), and the line won't route out to the industrial area (K3) → DONE [round1/r4-transit]: the roundabout Lotus Way leaves (Static Circus) was cut in two by zero-length street pieces with no lanes (left where the ring was split twice at one spot, where the Aurora Broadway tunnel passes under it), so nothing could drive round it to the one-way road; the lane build now joins the nodes such a piece links into one junction. Stops never snap to a tunnel under the street or a highway carriageway (slip roads and one-way streets take stops); a refused stop says why; a leg against a one-way street says "ONE-WAY" and how to fix it, instead of "NO ROAD".
- [x] A bus line refused to route round a roundabout with ramps between (K4) → DONE [round1/r4-transit]: same cause as K3 (Static Circus broken by two laneless zero-length ring pieces); legs round it now route (762 m round the ring in Harvey's day-29 save, was NO ROAD).

**Traffic and vehicles**
- [x] A tow truck took a wrong turn and a detour right at the accident: once close it should switch to working mode and go to the car (K21) → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: the stalled-car re-plan sent the recovery truck round its own wreck (a crash in the junction ahead), and nothing let it leave a detour when close. Now a truck going to a crash never re-plans round it, and within 70 m of its wreck with its route a detour (over 1.8x the distance + 30 m) or after standing 20 traffic s it switches to working mode: off the lanes at a crawl, ambers on, along the wreck's street (via the junction between them) to stop behind it, then hooks it (`World::work_mode`, `AfterMove::Recover`, `driving.work_mode_total`)
- [x] A garbage truck stuck going round in a circle (K23) → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: not reproduced (his day-29 save replayed to day 31 09:26 had no truck there; the sim diverges from his game). The motorcycle beside it was in the same queue

They now pull in to the kerb and traffic goes past (in a junction they still stop where they are), and a private car stopped at the kerb reads "pulled over at the kerb" (`StopReason::PulledOver`)
- [x] A crashed car awaiting a tow shows "passing through": it should say crashed/totalled, and cars behind should go round it (K45) → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: a crashed car is labelled "a wrecked suv" / "X's crashed sedan" (queue texts too) and its journey reads "written off in a crash, waiting for a tow" (or "a tow truck on its way", or "stopped after a crash"). His wreck stood in a junction, where nothing could pass: a junction's box now counts as room, the wreck is pushed aside and traffic eases past (`hazard.rs`; test `traffic_eases_past_a_wreck_in_a_junction`)
- [x] Parked cars in the middle of the road with traffic driving through them (K32, K35) → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: cars left with no slot (a hunter giving up, stranded by a rebuild, an old save) kept the place they had in their lane; a clamped car's fallback spot was 2.6 m to its left, on the centre line. Both now stand on the kerb line of the nearest street they can stand along, on the side nearer them (`World::squeezed_spot`; test `a_car_left_with_no_slot_stands_at_the_kerb_not_in_the_lane`). Old saves: such cars are re-found on load
- [x] A new road instantly full of parked cars that never drove there: cars must arrive and park, not appear (K41) → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: a road rebuild renumbers the street pieces but parked cars kept the old ids: the kerb index then marked slots taken on whatever streets had those numbers, every car failed its check and was re-parked slot by slot into the nearest free ones (the empty new road). Now a car whose slot is still where it stands keeps it under the new id (`remap_kerb_slots`, before anyone stranded by the rebuild looks for a slot; test `a_parked_car_keeps_its_kerb_slot_when_the_ids_change`). Left: cars whose street really went are still re-parked nearby without driving there
- [x] A car park says 2/109 taken but shows no cars: the count and the cars drawn must match (K44) → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: the cars on a lot are baked into its mesh, redrawn only when its fullness crossed an eighth (2/109 never did). Now every car redraws it while 12 or fewer stand there, then every 1/24 (`parking_band`)

**Construction and supply**
- [x] A new island's buildings started construction after a road was added, but no delivery trucks ever came (K6; Harvey thinks the on-ramp was poor). A site whose delivery trucks or construction company trucks can't reach it must not build: say so on the building ("delivery trucks can't get here", "builders can't reach this plot") with an alert and a mark on the plot. Today some construction starts before the first delivery truck arrives: construction should wait for its materials and its builders to arrive. Exceptions: homes on footpaths (residential lots reached only by a path: crews and deliveries come on foot or park on the nearest street), and a building extending its footprint onto land next to it when the building already has street access. → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: loads for a site with no route were dropped on it by magic (as were loads when the truck pool was full and loads on trucks stranded by a road edit), and the lorry from out of town always started at one road end, whatever network it was on. Now a site whose plot is on no street network that reaches a way into town does not build ("builders can't reach this plot"); a load no lorry can reach (tried from the four farthest ways in on the site's own network) is not delivered ("delivery trucks can't get here") and is sent again an hour later; a full pool or a road edit re-queues site loads. An alert (toast, Alerts list `construction_unreachable`), a red ring and label on the plot (`stub_marks.rs`), the inspector line and `under_construction.blocked` in the API. Exceptions as asked: homes on a footpath (crews come on foot; loads with no road are carried in), and a building spreading onto land next door (only its street plot is checked). Test `world/site_access_tests.rs`

**Trains and freight**
- [x] Freight trains don't carry real-looking shipping containers: one standard, realistic container everywhere (trains, ships, hubs, stacks homes) (K33) → DONE [round1/r4-transit]: one ISO model (`CONTAINER_20/40/W/H`: 6.06 / 12.19 x 2.44 x 2.59 m; corrugated sheet, rails, corner posts and castings, doors with locking bars), dry / reefer / tank / open-top builds and ten fictional shipping lines' liveries; used by the stacks homes, container yards and cargo hubs, freight yards, site offices, the freighters' deck cargo, the quay cranes' box, the lorries' trailers and the freight wagons (container flats).
- [x] A freight train stopped at its first car instead of pulling into the station; loading and unloading should be animated from its real contents (K33) → DONE [round1/r4-transit]: trains draw up with their head at the platform's far end (centred when shorter; into a spur to its buffers); imports ride as boxes per load (25 units a TEU, forty-footers where they fit, reefers for food, tanks for fuel); at the yard the gantry (bridge, trolley, spreader on the yard's runway beams) lifts that yard's boxes off onto the apron and the exports on, one by one, the stand lasting as long as the moves (`freight_dwell`). Remote: train `freight.containers`.
- [ ] Containers have a real life in the city: brought in on trains and ships, stored at stations and cargo hubs, taken out on trucks to where their goods go, used for other things, eventually reused as "stacks" homes; all visible, not props (K34)
  - Harvey 2026-10-28 additions: (1) every container carries its own number on the side (a real-style owner code + serial + check digit, e.g. "HFCU 482913 7", in its shipping line's livery), kept for its whole life, readable close up and in the inspector; it wears with age (paint fades, rust spreads, dents, patched panels, the number scuffed or painted over), so an old box looks old. (2) Containers are individual objects with a history: they arrive from all over the world like real ones (mixed lines, ages and conditions, not all fresh), each with an origin port and age. (3) Owners: shipping lines, companies and yards hold empties; they can store them, send them back out of town, sell them to stack developers/architects or to out-of-town buyers, or scrap them at a local scrap yard. (4) Stack developers can buy a box straight off the truck when its delivery is done, as well as from yards; a Stacks building is assembled from the actual boxes bought (their numbers, liveries and wear visible on the home). (5) Scrapped containers become a material (steel) the economy can use (scrap yard -> steel/parts). Inspector: a container's number, line, origin, age, condition, owner, where it has been, and what it is now (in transit, stored, a home, scrap).
- [x] A passenger train bumps into the freight train ahead, stops a second: trains keep a safe gap (K50); traffic and people stuck by the freight yard near the street (K51) → DONE [round1/r4-transit]: (K50) at 100 m a tick a train jumped past the 90 m block into the train ahead; trains now have a speed and brake (1.0 m/s²) to stand 30 m behind the train ahead, and for calls and the switch (Harvey's save, a day: min gap 1.2 m and 113 dead stops before, 30 m and 0 now). (K51) a freight train stood at the yard by the street (crossing at (316, -480)) with its tail over the level crossing the whole stand; a train never stands over a crossing at a call now (ticks the crossing was shut with a train standing at a call: 113 a day before, 23 now). The rest of the closures are freight trains every 45 min passing at yard speed.

**Drones**
- [x] A drone at "-" battery keeps circling instead of falling. Future air traffic is centrally scheduled: departures and landings timed so there is never a queue or circling (K28) → DONE [round1/r4-transit]: holding drained the battery with no floor (it went negative and kept circling). Every flight books its landing slot before it lifts off (a slot whose drone is leaving counts), waits on the pad for one (up to an hour), and taxis are sent to a pickup only with a slot there; a taxi short of charge diverts to the nearest pad with a slot or comes down in the street and charges off the recovery van; battery never below 0. Harvey's save, a day: drone-ticks holding 1911 -> 305, below 0 battery 76 -> 0.

## H. Public order and events (big features)
- [ ] See incidents as they happen (Harvey 2026-10-15). (1) A crime in progress shows a big red laser-like beam rising from its spot, visible from far off, until it's resolved. (2) The building inspector says when something is happening inside or at the building (today it doesn't, even with a police helicopter circling it): a clear INCIDENT line at the top (what, since when, units on scene, people involved) and a button that opens a popup with the police record for that incident (`services/incident.rs`: kind, stage, suspect, victims, callers, units, timeline, outcome). (3) Clicking the circling helicopter, or anything at the scene, leads to the same incident.
- [ ] Base jumping easter egg (Harvey 2026-10-15). Very rarely a daredevil citizen (reckless, young, bold) climbs a tall building (supertalls and towers; at dusk or early morning) and jumps off, a parachute opens and they glide down to land in a park, plaza or street, then walk off. Drawn as a small figure with a coloured canopy; a crowd may stop to look; the police may fine them if they land near; a rare one goes wrong (a tree, a hard landing, an ambulance). A news/Alerts line and a memory for the jumper and onlookers.
- [ ] Serial killer sprees (Harvey 2026-10-15). Very rarely a criminal citizen turns into a serial killer and goes on a spree lasting days: a killing now and then (using the existing murder/incident machinery in crime.rs and services/incident.rs), good at evading the police (searches come up empty, the case stays open), but always caught in the end. The finale is a standoff lasting hours at their home: police cars and officers in a ring around the building, lights flashing, a cordon, SWAT if it exists (ties to the SWAT item), then the arrest. The news and the people react: residents in the area are scared (mood down, fewer people out at night, concerns and memories), and the area's crime rate and fear stay up for a while after. The inspector and Alerts follow the case (victims, days at large, where it ended); the remote API exposes it.
- [ ] Riots, protests, parades taking over roads
- [ ] Police gunships, SWAT, armoured trucks, raids

## Brand
- [x] Rename the game to HEIFUNG://CITY (Harvey 2026-10-28; was "Neon District" / "District 2040") → DONE [rebrand]: window title, top bar, title logo, crash log, exe `heifung_city.exe` with icon and version info, docs, MCP tool text, committee prompts; citizens now "roll into" their city by its own name. Three procedural logo candidates in assets/brand/candidates (a blade sign, b terminal, c skyline); a is live, `make_logo.py --pick b|c` swaps. Internal names (nd_* crates, ND_* env vars) stay. Left: Harvey picks the logo.

## Extras (ideas, not scheduled; Harvey adds, starts only when he says)
- [ ] CSUR-style road lanes (Harvey 2026-10-26, idea from github.com/citiesskylines-csur/CSUR, a Cities: Skylines road framework: borrow the ideas only, no code or models; check its licence before quoting anything). Describe every road as lanes on a fixed half-lane grid from the centre line, so a road can be uneven (3 one way, 2 the other) and offset sideways, and lanes line up through an interchange. Generate the in-between pieces as road, not junctions: lane add/drop tapers, sideways lane shifts, and ramps as a road splitting or merging (today a ramp is "a one-lane one-way road joining with a yield"). Procedural markings to match: gore chevrons where ramps split, solid lines near ramps, lane-drop arrows and "lane ends" marks. A compact road code (e.g. "3+2 off 1") that is the lane layout, for presets, saves and the remote API. Touches traffic lane building (traffic/build.rs) and the road meshes; feeds the interchange stamp tool (section C) and the parclo/cloverleaf leftovers. Medium–large.

## Wave 1 (groups A–F parts) merged 2026-10-12. Left from wave 1: backland/F27 concave lots, parclo/cloverleaf ramps, save→load determinism, tow-job backlog (1,341 queued in Harvey's city: too few tow trucks → DONE [round1/r2-traffic]: on his day-62 save 2,210 calls queued and 8 towed a day. Causes: every car parked where it may not be became a tow call at once and none lapsed; three of his seven yards (24 staff) ran no trucks because their buildings were run down, yet counted as tow companies the city had; the pressure for a yard counted only tows done (capped by the out-of-town fleet). Now a run-down yard still runs its trucks (11 local + 4 out of town); a patrol books a tow only while fewer than 4 calls a truck wait (else a ticket only); an illegal-parking call no truck took in 6 h lapses; the yard pressure counts the calls waiting or turned down. Same save two days on: 180 calls (main: 2,358). Left: tows done a day stay low, the trucks crawl through his jams), car ferries have no real car queues. Wave 2 (G, H, rest of E/F) on hold.

- [ ] Crash in fresh generated cities around day 5-6: `invalid SlotMap key` at `traffic/crosswalk.rs:244` (`siren_near` reads a lane's `cars` list that still names a removed car). Seen four times in game on `generate_city` (grid_plaza seed 1 desert, twin_centres seed 2 islands temperate twice, ...) while stepping through the remote API; a save taken just before and loaded again runs on past it (the stale entry is not in the save), and headless `stale_lane_probe` (nd_procgen example) on `gen:grid_plaza:1` finds no stale entry in 9,000 ticks, so something on the app side (or a path the headless run never takes) leaves the lane list stale.
